   written, cached, and exposed on `resolveUnitForDate_`/`getWarmUpAnchor_`/
   `getAllUnits_`/`getUnitById_`. Fixing this also surfaced and fixed a
   real pre-existing bug: the pacing guide cache was already silently
   overflowing PropertiesService's 9216-byte-per-property limit with just
   the original 16 fields (~28KB measured against real data) — see
   HISTORY.md's resolution 8 for the full writeup and the per-unit-cache fix.
10. ~~`curriculum/PacingGuide_CAS_Context.csv` and `.docx` are stale~~ —
    **closed.** Both regenerated from the adopted v2 JSON — see
    HISTORY.md's resolution 8 for how each was rebuilt/verified. Prior versions archived at
    `curriculum/archived/PacingGuide_CAS_Context_v1_SUPERSEDED.csv`/`.docx` (since removed; on the `pre-archive-cleanup` branch).
11. **`data/CompetencyRegistry.csv` and `data/sol-correlations/` are not
    yet imported into any Sheet** — the files exist in the repo (HISTORY.md's resolution
    7), but nothing has run `importCompetencyRegistry()` (Script `22b`)
    against them in a live deployment. This is a deployment-time action,
    not a repo-file gap, listed here so it isn't mistaken for "already live."
    `importCourseData()` (script 51) now runs all three importers and
    `checkCourseData()` reports what is live; the SOL correlations and
    `LessonPrimarySecondary_Seed.csv` still have no importer.
12. ~~Confirmed duplicate top-level declarations across files sharing a
    GAS project~~ — **closed.** `gas-lint` found 6 distinct collisions;
    all 6 fixed and re-verified clean (0 errors, `node --check` passes
    repo-wide):
    - `SP_STUDENT_EMAIL`/`SP_TEACHER_EMAIL`/`SP_STUDENT_NAME`/`SP_SHADOW_MATRIX`:
      `25_WarmUpWriter.js`'s redundant "alias for readability" block
      deleted, its call sites now use the file's own already-unique
      `SP25_*` names directly.
    - `03_QueueBridge.js`'s `SP_QUEUE_ROW_REF`/`SP_STUDENT_FILE_ID`/`SP_CONFIG_ID`/`SP_TEACHER_EMAIL`/`SP_STATUS`
      (STAGING_PIPELINE columns) renamed to `STG_*` — these collided with
      `23_StudentProfileManager.js`'s StudentProfiles-column `SP_*`
      constants of the *same names but different values/meanings*
      (`SP_TEACHER_EMAIL` was 4 in one, 3 in the other).
    - `formatDateYMD_` (three identical copies in `23`/`24`/`25`):
      de-duplicated to the one definition in `23`, relied on via the
      shared Central Ledger project scope from `24`/`25`.
    - **`buildStudentRoster_`** (the serious one — two genuinely different
      functions sharing a name, `23_StudentProfileManager.js`'s
      `(ledgerData, teacherEmail, currentTerm)` vs.
      `29_StudentContextAggregator.js`'s `(ledgerSheet)`): `29`'s renamed
      to `buildValidatedStudentRoster_`, its one call site updated.
    - `extractFormEntryIds_` (`16_UnifiedManualSetup.js` vs.
      `16_UnifiedManualSetup_M5_ADDENDUM_v2.js`, which explicitly
      documented itself as replacing the base version but had never
      actually been merged): merged for real, plus the M6 addendum's
      one-line Lesson Unit extension on top. Both addendum files keep
      their now-inert content as commented-out historical record, not
      deleted.
13. **`03_QueueBridge.js`'s `bridgeQueue()` and `backPropagateCompletions()`
    now take a document lock** — closed. Both run on time triggers (1 min /
    2 min) and mutate `STAGING_PIPELINE`/`ReviewQueue`/`Ledger`; neither
    had any protection against Apps Script running two overlapping
    invocations if a run takes longer than its trigger interval (a real
    risk as those sheets grow), unlike their sibling
    `06_StagingPipeline_Turnstile.js`'s `runStagingTurnstile()`, which
    already locks for exactly this reason. Without a lock, two overlapping
    `bridgeQueue()` runs could both stage the same student submission,
    producing a duplicate AI evaluation; `backPropagateCompletions()` had
    a partial mitigation already (it re-reads the queue row's live status
    immediately before acting, rather than trusting its own stale read),
    which narrowed but didn't close the same race. Both now take
    `LockService.getDocumentLock()` with the same 15-second wait and
    congestion-standdown pattern the Turnstile already used, so a busy run
    stands down instead of racing.
14. **`31_PacingGuideManager.js`'s warm-up anchor cache was silently
    truncating text** — closed. `_loadPacingGuide_()` caches the whole
    pacing guide in Script Properties, and — because a single property
    value is capped at ~9KB — trimmed each unit's `warmup_anchor` to 200
    characters before caching, with no way to tell a genuinely-short
    anchor from one that got cut off mid-sentence. Every warm-up prompt
    built from a cache hit (the common case) silently used the truncated
    200-char version forever, even after the cache warmed up and a fuller
    read would have been cheap. Fixed by tagging each cached unit with a
    `warmup_anchor_truncated` flag at write time; `resolveUnitForDate_()`
    now threads that flag through, and `getWarmUpAnchor_()` checks it
    before returning — if set, it calls new helper
    `_getFullPacingField_(unit_id, fieldName)` to re-read that one unit's
    untruncated `warmup_anchor` directly from the `PacingGuide` sheet, so
    only the (rare) truncated case pays the extra read instead of every
    call. (This entry named the helper `_getFullWarmupAnchor_()` until
    `tools/doc-currency/check.js` pointed out that it does not exist —
    the real one is field-generic, not warmup-specific.)
15. **Setup wizard's three cross-project import calls, corrected fix
    (external product review, Finding 5)** — `28_Module2Setup.js` calls
    `importCompetencyRegistry()`/`importPacingGuide()`/
    `importCompetencyRubrics()` (Scripts 22b/31/32), all bound to the
    Central Ledger project, not `unified-manual` — genuinely separate
    Apps Script projects, confirmed via `tools/gas-lint/project-map.json`.
    The external review's own suggested fix ("expose them over the
    Ledger's existing `doPost` API") is factually wrong: `central-ledger`
    has no web-app surface — no `doGet`/`doPost` — at all today; the one
    `doPost` in cas-ccps lives in the separate `teacher-dashboard` project.
    **Real fix: an Apps Script Library**, the platform's own first-class
    mechanism for exactly this "share functions across bound projects"
    problem. `cas-ccps/clasp/manifests/unified-manual.appsscript.json` now
    declares a `dependencies.libraries` entry (`userSymbol: "CentralLedger"`),
    and all three call sites in `28_Module2Setup.js` now call
    `CentralLedger.importCompetencyRegistry()` etc. — resolved via
    `tools/gas-lint/check.js`'s own `checkUndefinedFunctionCalls`
    (the 3 warnings these calls used to produce are gone; `.`-prefixed
    calls are outside that check's scope by design, since it only flags
    bare identifier calls). Each call site keeps its existing
    `typeof ... !== "function"` guard (now checking `CentralLedger` itself)
    so a deployment that hasn't wired up the Library yet still gets the
    same graceful manual-fallback instructions instead of a bare
    ReferenceError. **What's left is entirely credentialed, same class of
    gap as the clasp connection itself (see "Version control (clasp)"
    below):**
    1. Open the live Central Ledger Apps Script project → Deploy → New
       deployment → select type **Library** → Deploy.
    2. Copy that deployment's Script ID and the version number it was
       just published at.
    3. Fill both into `unified-manual.appsscript.json`'s
       `dependencies.libraries[0]` (`libraryId`, `version`) — replacing
       the `REPLACE_WITH_...` placeholders — then `clasp push` the
       `unified-manual` project (or add the Library by hand via the
       Script Editor's Resources → Libraries UI, entering the same Script
       ID/version — either path produces the same result).
    4. Re-publish a new Library version and bump `version` in the manifest
       any time central-ledger's own code changes in a way that should
       reach `unified-manual` — an Apps Script Library is pinned to a
       specific version on purpose, so central-ledger changes never
       silently reach consumers without an explicit version bump.
16. **Scaling fixes (external product review, Finding 6, "this quarter")**
    — the review counted ~90 `getDataRange()` calls across cas-ccps;
    re-counted at 112 (worse, not better — per-file counts the review cited
    were exact, only the total was understated). Three fixes, each scoped
    to real, verified schema knowledge rather than a blanket rewrite:
    - **Bounded Ledger reads.** Added `LEDGER_COL_COUNT` (00_SharedConfig.js,
      one past the highest `LEDGER` index) and converted the 6 call sites
      across `10_AdminRecoveryPanel.js`, `29_StudentContextAggregator.js`,
      and `30_SCRSuggestionEngine.js` that read the *whole* Ledger tab
      (not a header-driven dynamic column set) from `getDataRange()` to
      `getRange(1, 1, lastRow, LEDGER_COL_COUNT)` — reads exactly the
      columns this schema actually defines, and isn't vulnerable to the
      well-known GAS gotcha where one stray far-right value (ever entered,
      even by accident) makes `getDataRange()` report a wider range than
      the real schema forever after. The other ~106 `getDataRange()` calls
      (against StagingPipeline, RubricQueue, SCRSuggestions,
      SCRDecisionLog, CompetencyEvidence, WarmUpResponses,
      StudentDocRegistry, and header-driven reads generally) were not
      individually converted in this pass — most either read a genuinely
      dynamic column set via header lookup (bounding those requires
      already knowing the header row's width, a chicken-and-egg problem)
      or a tab whose exact schema this pass didn't independently verify
      column-by-column; narrowing the scope here on purpose rather than
      risk silently truncating a column some other function actually needs.
    - **CacheService layer for CompetencyRegistry.** New
      `getCompetencyTextMap_()` (00_SharedConfig.js) — a real
      cross-execution cache (Apps Script's `CacheService`, 6-hour TTL),
      not a module-level variable that resets every execution. Replaces
      the identical `getDataRange()` + header-lookup block
      `30_SCRSuggestionEngine.js`'s `getSCRDashboardData_()` and
      `getStudentScrStandingForCompetencies_()` used to each build from
      scratch on every call. `22b_CompetencyRegistryImporter.js` now
      invalidates the cache entry on every successful re-import, so a
      newly-imported competency is visible immediately rather than
      waiting out the TTL. (The pacing guide — the review's other named
      caching target — already has its own, more sophisticated
      PropertiesService-backed per-unit cache; see Known Gaps #9/#14 above.
      Not touched here; a second, competing cache layer over the same
      data would be a regression, not an improvement.)
    - **Ledger retention, extending the `SCR_RETENTION_YEARS` pattern.**
      See `docs/FERPA_DATA_MAP.md`'s Retention section for the full
      writeup — `LEDGER_RETENTION_YEARS` (Script Property, default 5,
      explicitly unconfirmed against any real district retention
      schedule, same "correct the moment you know the real number"
      framing as `SCR_RETENTION_YEARS` itself) drives
      `_archiveExpiredLedgerRows_()` (`10_AdminRecoveryPanel.js`), run on
      the same daily/on-demand triggers as the SCRDecisionLog archival.
      This one needed a direct check against `FERPA_DATA_MAP.md` first —
      that document explicitly states it "does not assert a retention
      period that isn't actually enforced anywhere," which is exactly
      what inventing a Ledger retention policy would have done without
      this same unconfirmed-default framing; confirmed with the user
      before implementing on that basis.
17. **Flow 2 escape hatch (external product review, Finding 3, "this
    quarter")** — at the time this was written, Flow 2 (Student
    Evaluation) had never been built in Studio (see "Known gaps" above,
    now updated by Finding 18 below), and `15_StudioFlowPrompts.js`/
    `15b_StudioFlowPrompts_Flow2_Revised.js` are specs to paste into a
    Studio Gemini step, not runnable code — meaning nothing anywhere
    could actually exercise Flow 2's evaluation logic (prompt
    construction, response parsing, competency-evidence extraction)
    without a live Studio Flow. kos-personal already solved the general
    version of this with `CFG.INFERENCE_MODE` (`1_Config_And_Deploy.gs`)
    gating a fallback to its own separate, billed managed-inference-service
    (`kos-personal/inference-service/`) — cas-ccps gets an analogous
    opt-in escape hatch, scaled to what it actually needs: no separate
    deployment, just a direct Gemini API call.
    - `cfg.evaluationMode` (`00_SharedConfig.js`, default `"STUDIO"` —
      unchanged behavior) can be set to `"DIRECT_GEMINI"` via the
      `EVALUATION_MODE` Script Property, plus a `DIRECT_GEMINI_API_KEY`
      Script Property, to opt in.
    - `15b_StudioFlowPrompts_Flow2_Revised.js` moved into
      `cas-ccps:central-ledger`'s real file list (was previously excluded
      as "not deployed" in `tools/gas-lint/project-map.json`) so
      `FLOW_2_SYSTEM_PROMPT` has exactly one source of truth for both the
      Studio-paste path and the new direct-call path — see that file's
      own updated header comment for why this does NOT mean Flow 2 itself
      is now deployed in Studio (it still isn't).
    - New `15c_Flow2DirectEvaluationService.js`: `_buildFlow2Prompt_()`
      and `_parseFlow2Response_()` (pure logic, no network — reuses
      `04_Form2_TurnInGate.js`'s existing `scanCompliance_()`/
      `extractSuggestedScore_()` rather than duplicating them, and adds
      parsing for the `[MILESTONE_OUTCOMES: {...}]` line nothing else in
      this repo reads today) plus `runFlow2DirectGemini_()` (the thin
      orchestrator that actually calls `UrlFetchApp`) and
      `writeCompetencyEvidenceFromFlow2_()` (the CompetencyEvidence write
      step Flow 2 itself would otherwise perform, with the same
      "skip a milestone with a blank competency ID, never guess" rule
      `15b_StudioFlowPrompts_Flow2_Revised.js`'s own DEPENDENCY note
      specifies).
    - **Deliberately NOT wired into `06_StagingPipeline_Turnstile.js`'s
      automatic release loop.** Automatically rerouting live student
      submissions through an unreviewed new code path is a materially
      bigger decision than "make Flow 2 testable" calls for — see
      `15c_Flow2DirectEvaluationService.js`'s own header comment. Call
      `runFlow2DirectGemini_()` directly (Script Editor, or a manual
      admin action, with a real Gemini API key) to actually use this path.
    - See `tests/cas-ccps/flow2-direct-evaluation.test.js` for full
      coverage of the pure prompt-building/response-parsing/evidence-write
      logic, plus the mode-gating and error-handling of the one function
      that actually touches the network (with `UrlFetchApp` mocked, not a
      real Gemini call).
18. **Studio Steps adoption — the custom-step code Finding 17's escape
    hatch was working around now exists.** An 8th cas-ccps project,
    `cas-ccps/studio-steps/`, implements every custom step Flows 1-5
    genuinely need (native Studio connectors and Ask-Gemini steps cover
    everything else) — see its own README for the full file-to-flow map
    and the fixes applied while landing it. Also landed in the same
    effort: `34_QueueWatchdog.js` (WarmUpQueue/StagingPipeline/ReviewQueue
    monitoring with Chat-space escalation) and
    `35_FlowPreflightAndCanary.js` (a one-shot health check that, among
    other things, caught a real pre-existing gap — `CompetencyEvidence`
    was never created by any setup script, silently stranding Flow 2's
    evidence writes on a fresh deployment; now fixed in
    `createSCRTabs_()`), both added to `cas-ccps:central-ledger`. This
    closes Finding 17's underlying gap in code, but not in deployment —
    see Known Gap #1: the project exists and is tested, not yet pushed
    live. See `cas-ccps/HISTORY.md` for the full adoption record.

#### Naming note

Files prefixed `_ADDENDUM` (and `16_..._v2.js`, `15b_...Revised.js`,
`00_..._v2.js`) are patches — instructions for editing a base script, not
standalone deployable files. `scripts/09_StudentRevisionGuidance_M1Base.js`
and `scripts/07_TeacherDashboard.js`/`08_TeacherConfirmationStep.js`'s
pre-M2/M3 counterparts were **not** added under their original filenames
because this repo's existing `07`/`08` are strictly newer, additive
supersets of them — keeping only the newer copies avoids two files
silently claiming the same canonical name with one of them being stale.
`09` was the one exception, kept under both names (one archived), because
the difference there was architectural, not incremental — see
HISTORY.md's resolution 1.

API keys

Google account needed

📋

Planning a rollout to multiple teachers? Steps 1–9 below (plus the Student Dashboard, Final Properties, and Test steps) are one-time district setup — roughly ~2 hours total, done once no matter how many teachers use the system. Only one instruction, inside Step 10 ("Create the Teacher Dashboard project"), repeats — once per teacher, roughly ~10 minutes each — plus copying and sharing the Admin Manual with each teacher (see the final checklist).

Budget: ~2 hours + (~10 min × number of teachers). Planning for 8 teachers? That's about 3 hours and 20 minutes total, not 8 separate 2-hour deployments.

Phase 1 — Prepare

1

#### Confirm Prerequisites

Before you write a single line
5 min

1

###### Confirm your Google Workspace plan includes Workspace Studio

Workspace Studio (and the native Gemini connector) is available on Google Workspace for Education Plus, Business Starter and above, and Enterprise plans. Log into admin.google.com → Apps → Google Workspace → Workspace Studio, and verify it is enabled for your organization.

🚫
This is a hard requirement. The system has no fallback inference path. If Workspace Studio is not available on your plan, the evaluation pipeline cannot function.

2

###### Designate the admin Google account

Pick one Google account that will own all system assets — spreadsheets, folders, forms, and scripts. This account runs every automated process. It does not need to be a special admin account — any Google account with Drive access works. Use it for all remaining steps.

3

###### Verify you have Drive storage available

The system creates roughly 8–12 Google Drive assets during setup plus one folder per teacher and one document per student over time. A standard 15GB Drive account is sufficient for a school of any size since Google Docs, Sheets, and Forms don't count against Drive storage.

4

###### Have teacher email addresses ready

You'll need each teacher's email address to share their Teacher Manual copy. Students need no email — they use any Google account. Have at least one teacher email ready now for the test pilot in Step 12.

2

#### Download the Script Files

Gather everything before starting
5 min

1

You should have these files. Verify each is present before continuing. Archived files (prefixed ARCHIVED_) are not used — ignore them.

Phase 2 — Run the Wizard

3

#### Create the Admin Manual Document

The operational hub for the entire system
15 min

1

###### Create a new blank Google Doc

In your admin Google account, go to docs.google.com and create a blank document. Name it something like Assignment System — Admin Manual. This document becomes the operational hub — every teacher gets a copy of it.

2

###### Open the Apps Script editor

In the document menu bar, click Extensions → Apps Script. A new tab opens showing the script editor. You'll see one default file called Code.gs.

3

###### Create and paste four script files

For each file below, click the + button next to "Files" in the left panel, name it exactly as shown, then paste the corresponding script content.

00_SharedConfig

paste contents of 00_SharedConfig.js

16_UnifiedManualSetup

paste contents of 16_UnifiedManualSetup.js

19_ClonedSheetConfig

paste contents of 19_ClonedSheetConfig.js — required for writeConfigTab_()

20_SetupCheckpoint

paste contents of 20_SetupCheckpoint.js

Delete the default Code.gs file — right-click it → Delete.

⚠️
File names matter. Apps Script uses the file names as display labels and for debugging. Match them exactly, without the .js extension (Apps Script adds that automatically).

4

###### Save and close the editor

Press Ctrl+S (or Cmd+S on Mac) to save. Close the Apps Script tab. Return to your Google Doc.

4

#### Run the Setup Wizard

The wizard creates ~12 assets automatically
Automatic — ~60 sec

1

###### Reload the document

Close and reopen your Admin Manual Google Doc, or simply refresh the browser tab. After reloading, a custom menu will appear in the menu bar: ⚙️ Assignment System.

💡
If the menu doesn't appear after refreshing, open Extensions → Apps Script and run the onOpen function manually once to trigger authorization.

2

###### Click ⚙️ Assignment System → 🚀 Run Admin + Teacher Setup

A Google authorization dialog will appear. This is expected — click Review Permissions, choose your admin Google account, then click Allow. This authorizes the script to create Drive assets on your behalf.

3

###### Follow the wizard prompts

The wizard asks two questions for admin setup:

Prompt 1: Admin email address ← receives system alerts
Prompt 2: Organization name ← used in folder labels

Then three questions for your personal teacher setup:

Prompt 3: Your full name
Prompt 4: Your email address
Prompt 5: Your subject area ← e.g. Science, English

4

###### Wait ~60 seconds for asset creation

The wizard creates these assets automatically:

📁
Assignments folder + subfolders_Student Shared Folders / _System Templates

Automatic

📊
Central Ledger Spreadsheet5 tabs: Ledger, ReviewQueue, STAGING_PIPELINE, RubricQueue, MatrixRegistry

Automatic

📋
2 Master Template SheetsMASTER — Rubric Response Sheet / MASTER — Teacher Matrix Sheet

Automatic

📬
Central Turn-In Form (Form 2)Linked to the Central Ledger. Share this URL with all students.

Automatic

👤
Your personal teacher workspaceYour folder, matrix sheet, rubric form, intake form, and forms are created.

Automatic

5

###### Read the Setup Details pages

When the wizard finishes, two pages are appended to your Admin Manual: an Admin Setup Details page and a Teacher Setup Details page. Scroll to the end of the document now and read them. They contain direct links to every asset the wizard created.

✅
Save these links. The admin page has direct links to the Central Ledger, the two master template sheets, and the Assignments folder. You will need them in the next steps.

The admin page also includes a Known Limitations section. Read it now — it describes the forensic check strength, the pipeline blocking behavior, and batch size recommendations.

Phase 3 — Bind Scripts to Assets

⚡

Have Google Cloud Console access? The AutoInstaller (Script 21) can complete
Phase 3 automatically in ~3 minutes — no manual script pasting. It requires enabling the
Apps Script API in Cloud Console (console.cloud.google.com → APIs & Services → Library →
"Apps Script API" → Enable). If you have access, see REGISTRY_SHEET_SETUP.md
for setup instructions, then skip to Phase 4.

If you don't have Cloud Console access, follow the manual steps below — the outcome is identical.

5

#### Bind Scripts to the Central Ledger

7 scripts — one project — the operational hub
20 min

1

###### Open the Central Ledger Spreadsheet

Find the link in your Admin Setup Details page (end of your Admin Manual document). Click to open it — this is the spreadsheet the wizard created with five tabs.

2

###### Open Apps Script and create 7 files

Click Extensions → Apps Script. Delete the default Code.gs. Create one file for each script below — click + next to "Files", name it exactly as shown, paste the full contents, save.

00_SharedConfig

02_Form1_IntakeAndWorkspaceGenerator

03_QueueBridge

04_Form2_TurnInGate

06_StagingPipeline_Turnstile

10_AdminRecoveryPanel

18_FormSubmitDispatcher

3

###### Enable the Drive Advanced Service

In the left panel, click Services (+). Find Drive API and click Add. This enables the version history forensic check in Script 04.

4

###### Set one Script Property

Click Project Settings (gear icon) → Script Properties → Add script property:

ID_ADMIN_SPREADSHEET = [Central Ledger Spreadsheet ID]

The spreadsheet ID is the long string in its URL between /d/ and /edit.

5

###### Set 4 triggers

Click the clock icon ⏰ → + Add Trigger. Add these four, authorizing each when Google prompts:

Trigger 1dispatchFormSubmit — From spreadsheet — On form submit

Trigger 2bridgeQueue — Time-driven — Every 1 minute

Trigger 3backPropagateCompletions — Time-driven — Every 2 minutes

Trigger 4runStagingTurnstile — Time-driven — Every 1 minute

⚠️

Trigger 1 must call dispatchFormSubmit — not onFormSubmit. The name onFormSubmit is reserved by Apps Script and causes conflicts.

6

Clones to every teacher — scripts clone with it automatically
10 min

1

###### Open the Master Rubric Response Sheet

Find the link in your Admin Setup Details page. Named MASTER — Rubric Response Sheet in the _System Templates folder. Open it — you'll see a SETUP INSTRUCTIONS tab for reference.

2

###### Open Apps Script and create 3 files

Click Extensions → Apps Script. Delete Code.gs. Create:

00_SharedConfig
paste contents of 00_SharedConfig.js

05_TeacherIntakePipeline
paste contents of 05_TeacherIntakePipeline.js

19_ClonedSheetConfig
paste contents of 19_ClonedSheetConfig.js

Save. Do not set any triggers — Script 05's onOpen() self-registers the trigger when each teacher opens their cloned copy for the first time.

✅

Scripts bound to this master sheet clone automatically with every teacher copy. You do this once — every future teacher gets the scripts without any additional binding work.

7

#### Bind Scripts to the Master Teacher Matrix Sheet

Clones to every teacher — triggers self-register on first open
10 min

T

The compliance stamp is critical. The evaluation report must end with exactly [SYSTEM: APPROVED] or [SYSTEM: REVISION_REQUIRED] followed by ── END EVALUATION ──. Script 04's turn-in gate scans for these exact strings.

4

###### Test both flows now

Don't wait until Step 12 to find out whether these flows actually run — test each one directly, right now:

Flow 1: In the Central Ledger's RubricQueue tab, add one test row with real-looking rubric text and Status = PENDING_EXTRACTION. Within about a minute, its Status should flip to COMPLETE and a new DRAFT row should appear in the TeacherMatrix tab. Flow 2: In STAGING_PIPELINE, add one test row pointing at a real (test) student document with Status = IN_PROCESS. Within about a minute, that row's Status should flip to COMPLETE and the student document should have real feedback text inserted after ── FEEDBACK ──, ending in [SYSTEM: APPROVED] or [SYSTEM: REVISION_REQUIRED] and ── END EVALUATION ──. If not, re-check Flow 2's trigger condition and prompt mapping.

✅

⚠️

This project is deployed once per teacher, not once for the whole school — same convention as the Admin Manual (see the final checklist). Every server function on this dashboard scopes its data to whichever email is set below, and only lets that same person view it — so each teacher needs their own copy of this project, their own Script Property, and their own deployment URL. That's a separate one-time setup — a third Script Property (LEADER_HUB_OAUTH_CLIENT_ID) and a second deployment of this same project with different access settings — documented on its own in docs/LEADERHUB_CONNECTION_SETUP.md, not repeated here. 1

###### Set the current academic term

Open the Central Ledger Spreadsheet. In the menu bar, click ⚙️ Admin Controls → 📅 Set Current Term. Enter the current term identifier — for example 2025-26 S1. This tags every new student registration with the term so dashboards can filter by year and semester.

Update this at the start of each term. Past registrations are not affected — only new ones get the new term tag.

2

Scroll to the Teacher Setup Details page in your Admin Manual. You'll see two activation links under ⚡ Two Quick Activation Steps. Open each link once and click Allow when Google prompts you. These connect your rubric upload form and your assignment review system.

#### Run an End-to-End Test

Verify every component before going live
30 min

1

###### Upload a test rubric

Open your Rubric Upload Form (link in your teacher setup summary page). Submit a short but real rubric. Include a Google Doc URL as the prompt template — create a test doc with a few sentences first. You should receive a "Rubric Received" email within 30 seconds.

2

###### Confirm the review email arrives and confirm the assignment

Within a few minutes, you should receive a review email with a pre-filled confirmation link. Click it, verify the extracted fields look correct, and submit. You should then receive an activation email saying the assignment is LIVE.

If the review email doesn't arrive within 5 minutes, check the Central Ledger → RubricQueue tab. The row should show COMPLETE. If it shows PENDING_EXTRACTION, Studio Flow 1 did not trigger — verify the trigger condition in Studio.

3

###### Register a test student

Open your Student Registration Form (Form 1). Use a second Google account you control as the test student. After submitting, within 1–2 minutes a student document should appear in the test account's Shared With Me. Open it — verify it has the four zones (feedback header, prompt content, response area, CONFIG_ID footer).

4

###### Submit a test evaluation from the student document

Sign into the test student's Google account. Open their document. Write at least 25 words in the response zone. Click 📊 AI Evaluation Panel → Run Assignment Check. You should see a "Submitted for Feedback" confirmation. Within 1–3 minutes, feedback should appear at the top of the document.

If feedback doesn't appear within 5 minutes: check the Central Ledger → STAGING_PIPELINE tab. If a row is stuck IN_PROCESS, use ⚙️ Admin Controls → Reset Stuck Pipeline Row in the Central Ledger spreadsheet.

5

###### Test the Turn-In Form

Once the student document shows a [SYSTEM: APPROVED] stamp, open the Central Turn-In Form URL (in your Admin Setup Details). Submit using the test student's Google account identifier and the document URL. The teacher (you) should receive a verified submission notification email.

6

###### Check both dashboards

Open the Teacher Dashboard URL — verify the test student appears with status COMPLIANT. Open the Student Dashboard URL while signed in as the test student — verify their assignment appears with a submitted status.

✅
If all six checks pass, the system is fully operational and ready for teacher onboarding.

#### System is Deployed

The infrastructure is live. Before you share teacher manuals,

- Admin Manual has 4 scripts bound: 00_SharedConfig, 16_UnifiedManualSetup, 19_ClonedSheetConfig, 20_SetupCheckpoint

## KOS_CODE_CAS_CCPS_DOCS · cas-ccps/docs/CAS_ContextualGates_DesignPrinciples.html

File: `cas-ccps/docs/CAS_ContextualGates_DesignPrinciples.html`

CAS · Design Principles

Philosophy
The Five Gates
Patterns
Anti-Patterns
Implementation
Checklist

Classroom Agency System · UI/UX Design Principles

### Contextual
Gates

A philosophy for building setup wizards and deployment flows that fail loudly, verify everything, and never defer instructions to a document the user won't read.

0

Silent failures

0

Deferred instructions

0

Unknown states

∞

In-place retries

The principle

#### Fail loudly.
At the right moment.

Most setup wizards treat completion as a binary: the script ran without errors, therefore setup is done. This is wrong. A script can run without errors and leave a system in a state that doesn't work — and the user won't find out until they're in front of a class and the "New Lesson" button doesn't appear.

A "complete" state means verified complete — not "the script ran without errors."

Contextual gates is the design practice that enforces this. Every action that has a dependency, a manual follow-up, or a correctness risk stops the user at the exact moment it matters — verifies the result, surfaces any gap, and gives a path forward. Nothing is deferred to a summary document. Nothing fails silently. The user always knows exactly what state the system is in.

The wizard should leave the teacher in a known, verified, unambiguous state at every step — and when it can't, it should make the gap visible at the point where it matters, not in a document they'll never read.

CAS Design Principle · Script 28 development

❌ The old pattern

###### Deferred failure

Setup runs. Summary doc is appended. Teacher clicks through the wizard, sees "Setup Complete," and closes the document. Three days later they open the Teacher Dashboard. No "New Lesson" button. They don't know why. They don't know what to fix. They email the admin.

vs

✓ The contextual gate

###### Verified completion

After Phase A completes, the alert shows exact redeploy steps — model, step count, what to click — before the teacher closes the wizard. The completion state is: dashboard redeployed, competency tabs verified, backfill trigger confirmed. The teacher leaves knowing exactly what they have.

The practice

#### Five gates.
One principle.

Every step in a setup wizard should pass through all five gates before declaring success. Gates are not optional — they are the completion condition, not a defensive afterthought.

01

##### Verify your own output

After every creation or write operation, read it back and confirm it landed correctly. Don't assume success because no exception was thrown. A tab creation call can succeed without the tab appearing if the spreadsheet has quota issues. A trigger installation can succeed without the trigger firing if permissions are incomplete.

The verification is the completion condition. The step is not done when the write call returns — it's done when the read-back confirms the expected state.

02

##### Surface failures at the point of action

When a step fails, the alert appears at that step — not at the end, not in the summary doc, not in a log the user never sees. The failure message names exactly what failed, explains the most likely cause, and gives the path forward in the same alert.

A failure message that says "An error occurred" is not a failure message. It is noise. A failure message that says "The CompetencyRegistry tab could not be created — verify you have edit access to [URL]" is a gate.

03

##### Retry in-place, never restart

When user input fails validation, the wizard stays open and presents the same prompt again. It does not exit, direct the user to a menu item, or require a full restart. Re-entry is in-place. The failure message appears before the next prompt — the user understands what they need to fix before they see the input field.

Three attempts is the standard ceiling. On the third failure, the wizard exits with a clear message explaining which menu item to use to complete the step. It never silently drops the requirement.

04

##### Never ask for what the system already knows

Before prompting the user to type a value, check whether that value already exists in the system. Course names live in the CompetencyRegistry tab and in Script Properties. Teacher name and email live in Script Properties from Module 1 setup. The admin root folder ID lives in Script Properties set at admin setup time.

Present found values to the user for confirmation — not for re-entry. Confirmation is one click. Re-entry is a correctness risk. The system should never require the user to type something it already has.

05

##### Make deferred state visible and persistent

If a step is deferred — because the user skipped it, because a file wasn't available, because a dependency wasn't met — that state must be visible every time the user opens the system until it's resolved. The menu item changes. The dashboard shows a banner. The status function reports it explicitly.

A "deferred" state that is indistinguishable from a "complete" state is a silent failure in a different costume. Deferred is acceptable. Silent is not.

How it looks in practice

#### The patterns

Concrete implementations of the five gates in Google Apps Script. These are not abstract guidelines — they are copy-paste starting points for any wizard step.

##### Pattern 1 — Verify after create

Create the resource, then read it back immediately. Gate blocks on mismatch before proceeding.

Tabs created — then verified before moving to next step
Apps Script

// Create
_createM2LightweightTabs_(ss);

// Verify — read back immediately
const missingTabs = ["LessonContext", "CompetencyRegistry", "AlignmentLog", "ReportRegistry"]
.filter(t => !ss.getSheetByName(t));

if (missingTabs.length > 0) {
ui.alert(
"✗ Tab Creation Failed",
"Could not create:\n\n" +
missingTabs.map(t => " • " + t).join("\n") + "\n\n" +
"Verify you have edit access to:\n" + ss.getUrl(),
ui.ButtonSet.OK
);
return; // Gate: do not proceed
}
// Only reaches here if ALL tabs verified present

##### Pattern 2 — In-place retry loop

User input is validated inline. Failure shows the exact problem and re-presents the same prompt. Wizard never exits on recoverable error.

Schedule input — validates inline, retries in-place, exits cleanly after 3 failures
Apps Script

for (let attempt = 0; attempt 3; attempt++) {
const resp = ui.prompt(
"Block Schedule" + (attempt > 0 ? " — Fix and Retry" : ""),
FORMAT_HELP,
ui.ButtonSet.OK_CANCEL
);

if (resp.getSelectedButton() !== ui.Button.OK) return null;

const { rows, errors } = parseSchedule_(resp.getResponseText());

if (errors.length > 0) {
ui.alert(
"⚠ Schedule Format Errors" +
(attempt 2 ? " — Please fix and retry" : " — Final attempt"),
errors.map(e => " • " + e).join("\n"),
ui.ButtonSet.OK
);
if (attempt === 2) return null; // Clean exit after 3 failures
continue; // Stay in loop — re-present same prompt
}

// Validation passed — confirm before writing
const confirm = ui.alert("✅ Confirm", preview(rows), ui.ButtonSet.OK_CANCEL);
if (confirm !== ui.Button.OK) continue; // Re-entry, not restart

writeSchedule_(rows);
return { rows }; // Success
}

##### Pattern 3 — Read before asking

Check every available data source before prompting. Present found values for confirmation, not re-entry. Only ask when the system genuinely doesn't know.

Course name resolution — three sources tried before manual entry
Apps Script

// Source 1: already in the registry tab?
const fromRegistry = readCoursesFromRegistry_(ss);
if (fromRegistry.length > 0) {
const ok = ui.alert("✓ Courses Found", fromRegistry.join("\n"), ui.ButtonSet.OK_CANCEL);
if (ok === ui.Button.OK) return fromRegistry;
}

// Source 2: stored in Script Properties from a previous run?
const fromProps = props.getProperty("M2_COURSES")?.split(",") ?? [];
if (fromProps.length > 0) {
const ok = ui.alert("✓ Courses in Config", fromProps.join("\n"), ui.ButtonSet.OK_CANCEL);
if (ok === ui.Button.OK) return fromProps;
}

// Source 3: only now ask the teacher — and use retry loop
return promptForCourseNames_(ui);

##### Pattern 4 — Inline instructions at the moment of action

Any manual step the user must take after the wizard runs is shown in the completion alert — not in the summary doc. The instructions are specific enough to follow without leaving the screen.

Completion alert — redeploy instructions inline, not deferred to doc
Apps Script

ui.alert(
"✅ Phase A Complete",

// What was verified
"✓ Tabs created and verified.\n" +
"✓ " + importResult.rowCount + " competencies imported.\n" +
"✓ Backfill trigger confirmed active.\n\n" +

// ── Inline instruction — not in the doc ──────────────────
"━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━\n" +
"ACTION REQUIRED — Redeploy Teacher Dashboard\n" +
"━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━\n" +
" 1. Open the Script 07 project\n" +
" 2. Deploy → Manage deployments\n" +
" 3. Edit → New version → Deploy\n\n" +
"Your dashboard URL stays the same:\n" + dashUrl,

ui.ButtonSet.OK
);

##### Pattern 5 — Persistent deferred state in the menu

A deferred step changes the menu item label until it's resolved. The user sees the gap every time they open the document — not just once in a completion alert they dismissed.

Menu construction — four distinct states, each visible and actionable
Apps Script (add to Script 16 onOpen)

const m2PhaseA = props.getProperty("M2_SETUP_PHASE_A_COMPLETE");
const m2Full = props.getProperty("M2_SETUP_COMPLETE");
const m2Ready = props.getProperty("M2_REGISTRY_IMPORTED");

menu.addSeparator();

if (!m2PhaseA) {
// State 1: not started
menu.addItem("📚 Set Up Lesson Intelligence", "runModule2Setup");

} else if (!m2Ready) {
// State 2: started but import deferred — gap is visible every open
menu.addItem("⚠ Module 2 — Import Required", "runModule2ImportOnly");

} else if (!m2Full) {
// State 3: Phase A complete, Phase B available
menu.addItem("🌅 Set Up Warm-Up Generation", "runModule2WarmUps");

} else {
// State 4: fully operational
menu.addItem("📚 Open Lesson Dashboard", "openLessonDashboard");
}

What to avoid

#### The anti-patterns

These are the failure modes that contextual gates are designed to prevent. Each one produces the same outcome: a user who doesn't know the system isn't working until they need it to work.

Silent success

The script ran without errors, therefore setup is done. No verification of the output state. Tabs created but empty. Triggers installed but not authorized. Registry imported but wrong format. The user sees a completion message and walks away.
Fix → Gate 1: Verify output before declaring step complete.

Summary doc delivery

Instructions appear in the summary doc appended at wizard end. Teachers close the wizard when they see "Setup Complete." They don't read the page that was appended. The redeploy step, the manual trigger authorization, the CSV upload — all missed.
Fix → Gate 2: Surface every required action in the completion alert, not the doc.

Exit on bad input

Validation fails and the wizard exits. The user has to find the menu item, re-open the wizard, navigate back to the failed step, and try again — with no memory of what they entered before. On the second attempt they often make the same mistake.
Fix → Gate 3: Retry in-place. Stay in the loop. Show the error before the re-prompt.

Redundant entry

The wizard asks the teacher to type a value the system already has. Course names typed manually must match the CSV exactly — a trailing space breaks the dropdown silently. Teacher name typed again when Module 1 setup already stored it.
Fix → Gate 4: Read before asking. Present found values for confirmation, not re-entry.

Silent deferral

A skipped step is indistinguishable from a completed step. The teacher defers the CSV import. The system marks Phase A complete. The competency dropdown is empty. The teacher logs a lesson and sees no competencies to select — no error, no indication, no path forward.
Fix → Gate 5: Deferred state changes the menu label. Every open is a reminder.

Premature completion

The wizard offers a feature before the dependencies that make it useful exist. Phase B (warm-up generation) offered at the end of Phase A, before any evaluation history exists. Teacher sets up warm-ups, triggers run nightly against empty profiles, generic prompts are generated. Feels like it's working but isn't doing anything meaningful.
Fix → Gate the offer on data readiness. Activate Phase B when M1 evaluation history exists.

Design decisions

#### The tradeoffs

Contextual gates add friction to the wizard flow. More alerts, more read-backs, more steps. These tradeoffs are deliberate.

Decision | Tradeoff accepted | Rationale |

Verify after every create |
Extra read API calls. Slightly slower. |
A failed silent create caught at setup costs 2 seconds. Caught in the classroom costs a lesson. |

3-attempt retry loop |
Three possible failures before exit. User can feel stuck. |
Most users succeed on retry 1 or 2. Clean exit on attempt 3 with a specific menu item is better than silent failure after attempt 1. |

Inline redeploy instructions |
Long completion alert. Not dismissible quickly. |
The teacher who doesn't read it still saw it. The teacher who reads the summary doc would have seen it there — this just ensures both paths converge. |

_gateAlert_ as no-op for progress messages |
Less visual progress feedback between steps. |
Alerts require clicks. Click fatigue is real. Progress messages that require no action don't need a dismiss button — they go to the log. |

Menu label reflects deferred state |
Teachers see a warning item in their menu until they complete the step. |
That's the point. The warning is the behavior. Removing the warning requires completing the step. |

Summary doc as record, not instructions |
Summary doc has less actionable content than before. |
The summary doc was never a reliable delivery mechanism for instructions. Making it a record (what was configured, when, by whom) makes it useful for its actual audience: the admin auditing the installation. |

◈
The summary doc is not retired — it's repurposed. Summary pages still get appended to the manual on every setup run. But their job changes: they're an audit record for administrators, not a delivery mechanism for teacher instructions. The distinction matters. An admin reviewing what was configured six months ago needs the doc. A teacher configuring their classroom for next week needs the inline alert.

◈
This principle applies beyond setup wizards. Any Apps Script flow that creates something, deploys something, or configures something should apply the same gates. Module 1's AutoInstaller, Module 1's rubric extraction confirmation step, Studio Flow error handling — all of them have silent failure modes that contextual gates would catch. Script 28 is the proof of concept. The pattern scales to every deployment-adjacent flow in the system.

Before shipping

#### The checklist

Apply to every wizard step before shipping. If any item is unchecked, the step has a silent failure mode.

###### Gate 1 — Verification

Output is read back after every write. Tab created → tab read back. Trigger installed → trigger list checked. Row written → row count verified.

Verification failure blocks progress. The step is not marked complete if the read-back doesn't match the expected state.

Verification failure gives the URL. Every tab/spreadsheet verification failure includes the spreadsheet URL in the alert text.

###### Gate 2 — Failure messages

No failure message says only "An error occurred." Every failure names exactly what failed.

Every failure gives the most likely cause. Not just what happened — why it probably happened.

Every failure gives a path forward. Either "try again" or the exact menu item / manual step to resolve it.

###### Gate 3 — Retry behavior

Validation failure stays in the wizard. No prompt validation exits the wizard — it re-presents the same prompt.

Retry prompt shows the error before the input. The teacher knows what to fix before they see the field again.

Three-failure exit is clean and specific. After 3 attempts, wizard exits with the exact menu item to retry from.

###### Gate 4 — Data reuse

No value is asked for that the system already has. Script Properties, existing tab data, and config files are checked before any prompt.

Found values are presented for confirmation, not re-entry. One click to confirm. Not a text field.

###### Gate 5 — Deferred state

Every deferred step changes a menu item. The gap is visible on every document open until resolved.

Deferred state is stored in a distinct Script Property. Not inferred from absence — explicitly set as a known incomplete state.

Completing a deferred step clears the warning. The menu item reverts to the operational state label when the step is resolved.

###### The summary doc

No action items appear only in the summary doc. Every required action is in the completion alert. The summary doc records what was configured.

Manual steps are in the completion alert with exact steps. Not "see the summary doc." The steps are in the alert itself.

Contextual Gates
Classroom Agency System · UI/UX Design Principles · v1.0
Derived from Script 28 development · CAS Module 2 Setup Wizard
