Studio Flow Specification

### Flow 3Warm-Up Generation

Student-specific cognitive entry points generated from lesson context and individual learning history. Four prompt archetypes. One doc per student per class day.

Trigger: WarmUpQueue PENDING
6am class-day cron
Gemini · temperature 0.7
~30s per student
Anchor-aware · v1.1
Shadow matrix · pacing guide

What this flow does

#### One prompt, one student, one class

Flow 3 reads a WarmUpQueue row containing two JSON snapshots — the lesson context and the student's profile — and generates a single warm-up prompt personalized to that student's learning history. It creates a Google Doc in the student's warm-up folder, stamps it with the correct zone structure, shares it, and marks the queue row DELIVERED.

◈
The core design constraint: The warm-up is not a review question and not a preview question. It is a cognitive entry point — something that makes the student think before the lesson begins, creates a productive tension that the lesson resolves, and is interesting enough that a student who doesn't have to engage probably will anyway. It should never feel like homework.

Parameter | Value | Rationale |

Model | gemini-pro | Sufficient for structured creative generation. gemini-ultra overkill at this scale. |

Temperature | 0.7 | High enough for genuine variation between students. Low enough to stay on-topic and coherent. |

Max output tokens | 400 | 3–5 sentences of prompt + doc zone scaffolding. No more needed. |

Output format | Structured text | Not JSON — Flow 3 writes narrative prose directly into the doc. Structure enforced by zone delimiters. |

Trigger | WarmUpQueue status = PENDING | Script 24 writes PENDING rows directly at 3:30am for students with no prior warm-up history — or writes PENDING_BRIDGE instead for a returning student, which Flow 5 (Bridging Flow, below) promotes to PENDING once it writes bridge_output. Flow 3's own trigger condition is the same either way; it never watches PENDING_BRIDGE itself. Flow 3 processes PENDING rows at 6am. |

Per-teacher lane | Yes — same as Module 1 | Flow 3 processes one PENDING row at a time per teacher lane. Prevents queue flooding at 6am. |

Input

#### What Flow 3 receives

Flow 3 reads two columns from the WarmUpQueue row: lesson_context_snapshot and student_profile_snapshot. Both are JSON strings serialized by Script 24 at 3:30am. Flow 3 parses them and constructs the generation prompt from their contents.

lesson_context_snapshot — full structure

{
"lesson_id": "LES-20260114-A3F2",
"lesson_date": "2026-01-14",
"objective": "Students can explain how exclusivity affects perceived brand value in sports marketing.",
"activity": "Case study analysis of Nike's limited-release strategy and its effect on resale markets.",
"vocabulary": "exclusivity, perceived value, scarcity, brand equity, resale market",
"prior_connection": "Last class covered how branding creates emotional associations with consumers.",
"competency_ids": ["8175-47", "8175-52"],
"competency_texts": [
{ "id": "8175-47", "text": "Explore the concept of branding.", "strand": "Exploring the Concept of Branding" },
{ "id": "8175-52", "text": "Describe product development strategies.", "strand": "Describing Product Development" }
],
"course_name": "Sports Entertainment and Event Marketing",
"period": "2"
}

student_profile_snapshot — full structure

{
"student_email": "student@school.edu",
"student_name": "Jordan", // first name only for prompt naturalness
"period": "2",
"competencies_addressed": ["8175-1", "8175-2", "8175-38", "8175-45"], // IDs covered this term
"competency_gaps": ["8175-52"], // today's competencies NOT yet addressed for this student
"evaluation_signals": [
{
"assignment_id": "VDOE-XK7R-2026",
"date": "2026-01-10",
"indicators": {
"strengths": ["analysis", "critical_thinking"],
"gaps": ["application", "supporting_evidence"]
},
"note": "Strong conceptual grasp but struggles to apply terminology to real scenarios."
}
],
"warmup_scores": [
{ "lesson_id": "LES-20260110-B2C1", "engagement": 3, "total": 9 },
{ "lesson_id": "LES-20260108-A1D4", "engagement": 2, "total": 7 }
],
"extra_credit_count": 1,
"avg_engagement_score": 2.5,
"last_updated": "Fri Jan 13 2026 03:00:12 GMT-0500",
// Say/Do Ledger cas-ccps Extension 1 (SCR-to-warmup bridge) — added after this example was last updated:
"scr_standing_note": "Current SCR standing on today's competencies — 8175-47: 3/5 (teacher-confirmed); 8175-52: 4/5 (AI-suggested, not yet reviewed by the teacher)."
}

◈
First name only in the prompt. Flow 3 addresses the student by first name in the warm-up doc header. The profile snapshot contains the full name — Flow 3 extracts the first word. This makes the doc feel personal without being surveillance-adjacent. The full name appears only in the doc filename, not in the prompt text itself.

◈
SCR standing note (Say/Do Ledger cas-ccps Extension 1) — added, not yet consumed. scr_standing_note is computed by getStudentProfileSnapshot_() in Script 23 from a new bridge function in Script 30 (getStudentScrStandingForCompetencies_()), scoped to today's resolved pacing unit's own competency IDs — a different signal from competencies_addressed/competency_gaps above, which come from teacher-checked Lesson Context entries. Null when the student has no SCR rating on record for any of today's competencies. Same "passed through, no prompt-template placeholder yet" status as getWarmUpAnchor_()'s own chain_node/esports_connection v2 fields (31_PacingGuideManager.js) — intended as a soft signal/tie-breaker in a future Mode A/B prompt update, not something Flow 3 currently reads.

Personalization logic

#### Four prompt archetypes

Flow 3 selects one of four prompt angles based on the student's profile. The selection is deterministic — given the same profile, the same archetype is always chosen. The content within the archetype varies with temperature.

Archetype selection now uses a two-layer model. The shadow matrix is checked first — if cross-unit confidence has crossed 0.75 for a student × archetype combination, that archetype is used directly. If confidence is below threshold or no shadow matrix data exists, the decision table below applies. The decision table is the fallback, not the primary selector for students with established history.

Archetype | Trigger condition (decision table — used when shadow confidence < 0.75) | Shadow matrix override |

PARADOX |
avg_engagement ≥ 2.5 AND strengths contain analysis or critical_thinking AND gaps contain application |
Used directly when shadow cross_confidence ≥ 0.75 for PARADOX on this student in the current unit type |

CONCRETE SCENARIO |
avg_engagement ≥ 1.5 AND gaps contain analysis or critical_thinking AND strengths contain application |
Used directly when shadow cross_confidence ≥ 0.75 for CONCRETE SCENARIO |

BRIDGE |
competency_gaps is non-empty AND today's lesson introduces a duty area not yet covered this term |
Shadow matrix weights toward BRIDGE for early-unit warm-ups (within_confidence < 0.3) regardless of decision table |

PROVOCATION |
avg_engagement ≥ 2.5 AND extra_credit_count ≥ 1 AND evaluation_signals show no persistent gaps |
Used directly when shadow cross_confidence ≥ 0.75 for PROVOCATION |

◈
Shadow matrix selection note in Mode A prompt: When the shadow matrix overrides the decision table, Flow 3 receives a {shadow_archetype_note} placeholder explaining why: "Shadow matrix suggests PARADOX (72% confidence, 5 warm-ups in unit S1-U3, trend: rising)." This string is computed by getStudentProfileSnapshot_() in Script 23 at 3:30am queue-build time — not at generation time — and embedded in the student_profile_snapshot JSON on the WarmUpQueue row. Flow 3 reads it from the queue row with no additional Sheets reads. Null when cross_confidence < 0.75 or no shadow history exists for the current unit. Snapshot isolation fully preserved.

◈
Fallback order when no archetype matches (decision table): BRIDGE → CONCRETE SCENARIO → PARADOX. A student with no evaluation history and no warm-up history receives a BRIDGE prompt — all competencies are technically gaps and the anchor's prior connection field provides the bridge material. The pacing guide's prior_lesson_connection ensures BRIDGE has meaningful content even on the first day of a new unit.

Archetype 01 · Paradox

##### Surfaces contradiction

For students who think deeply but stay at the conceptual level. Presents two things that both seem true but can't both be right — at least not without nuance the lesson provides. The student walks in already wrestling with the tension.

Nike's Air Jordan line became one of the most valuable brands in sports history — but Nike deliberately limits how many pairs get released. If fewer people can buy your product, how can it be more valuable? What does that tell you about what "value" actually means in a market?

Archetype 02 · Concrete Scenario

##### Grounds abstraction

For students who work well with tangible problems but struggle to lift to the conceptual level. Presents a specific, real-sounding situation that requires judgment. The abstraction emerges from the scenario, not the other way around.

You're managing sponsorship for a mid-size esports team. A major energy drink brand offers you a deal — but their product has faced public criticism for targeting underage consumers. The money would fund your next three tournaments. What do you do, and why?

Archetype 03 · Bridge

##### Connects prior to new

For students entering a duty area they haven't covered yet. Finds the closest competency they have addressed and builds a bridge. Reduces the cognitive cost of encountering genuinely new material by activating adjacent knowledge.

Last week you looked at how branding creates emotional associations — the feeling a logo or name gives you. Today we're going into how companies develop products around those feelings. Before we start: think of a sports brand you feel something about. What did they have to get right about their product for that feeling to work?

Archetype 04 · Provocation

##### No easy answer

For students who are strong across the board and have demonstrated they'll engage beyond the minimum. A genuine intellectual challenge that doesn't scaffold. Rewards prior knowledge. Designed to make the discussion interesting for the whole class.

A sports team's brand can be worth more than the team itself — the New York Yankees brand generates more revenue than most franchises earn from games, merchandise, and media combined. If that's true, what exactly is a sports team selling? Is it games, or is it something else entirely?

Prompt engineering

#### Two-mode system prompt

Flow 3 operates in two modes depending on whether a warmup_anchor is present in the lesson context snapshot. When an anchor exists (pacing guide imported), Flow 3 personalizes it. When no anchor exists (first run, pacing guide not yet loaded), Flow 3 generates from scratch. Both modes use the same archetype selection logic and writing constraints. The output is indistinguishable to the student.

◈
What the anchor changes: Without an anchor, Flow 3 must simultaneously determine the pedagogical angle, generate the industry example, and calibrate the difficulty — three generative tasks at once. With an anchor, the teacher has already resolved the pedagogical angle and industry grounding. Flow 3's only job is to adapt the angle to this specific student's history. The anchor guarantees pedagogical quality; Flow 3 guarantees personalization.

##### Mode A — Anchor-Aware when warmup_anchor is present

Flow 3 receives the teacher-authored anchor and personalizes it for this specific student. The anchor provides the pedagogical angle and industry grounding. Flow 3 applies the archetype, adjusts the cognitive demand, and targets the student's history — without rewriting the anchor's core idea.

###### System Prompt — Mode A (Anchor-Aware)

Use when warmup_anchor != null in lesson_context_snapshot

You are personalizing a warm-up prompt for a high school CTE student in a Sports, Entertainment, and Event Management/Marketing course.

YOUR ROLE:
A teacher has written a seed prompt — a warmup anchor — that establishes the pedagogical angle and industry grounding for today's class. Your job is to adapt that anchor for this specific student based on their learning history. You are not rewriting the anchor. You are adjusting its cognitive demand, emphasis, and framing to match where this student is right now.

The final prompt must feel like it was written for this student specifically — not like a template that was filled in.

TEACHER'S ANCHOR PROMPT:
{warmup_anchor}

UNIT CONTEXT:
Unit: {pacing_unit_name} (Stage {pacing_stage})
Course objective for this unit: {course_objective}
Prior lesson connection: {pacing_prior_connection}
Key vocabulary in play: {pacing_key_vocabulary}

TODAY'S LESSON:
Course: {course_name}
Objective: {objective}
Competencies addressed: {competency_texts_formatted}

STUDENT CONTEXT:
Name: {first_name}
Competency gaps today (areas not yet covered): {competency_gaps_formatted}

Evaluation history (last 3 assignments):
{evaluation_signals_formatted}

Warm-up engagement history:
Average engagement score: {avg_engagement_score} / 3.0
Extra credit replies submitted: {extra_credit_count}

Shadow matrix — archetype confidence for this unit:
{shadow_archetype_note}

ARCHETYPE SELECTED: {archetype}

How to apply each archetype to the anchor:
PARADOX — The anchor already contains a tension. Sharpen it. Make the contradiction more explicit for a student who processes ideas conceptually. Don't resolve it — that's what class is for.
CONCRETE SCENARIO — The anchor is abstract. Ground it. Add a specific, real-sounding situation that a student who thinks in examples can grab onto before they lift to the concept.
BRIDGE — The anchor assumes prior exposure. Add a sentence that connects today's anchor to something this student has already covered, named specifically from their competency history.
PROVOCATION — The anchor is too comfortable for this student. Raise the stakes. Add an implication or an edge case that has no easy answer and rewards exactly the kind of knowledge this student has been building.

ADAPTATION CONSTRAINTS — follow all of these without exception:
1. Preserve the anchor's core question and industry example. Do not replace them.
2. You may add 1–2 sentences of framing before or after the anchor.
3. The final prompt is 3 to 5 sentences total including any framing you add.
4. Never use a list, bullet point, or numbered items.
5. Exactly one open question — the anchor's question, sharpened if needed.
6. Never mention grades, points, scores, or evaluation.
7. Never use the words "today" or "lesson."
8. Never reference the student's past performance explicitly.
9. Write in second person ("you", "your").
10. If the anchor already perfectly fits the archetype and student — return it unchanged. Do not adapt for the sake of adapting.

OUTPUT FORMAT:
Return only the adapted warm-up prompt text. No preamble, no label, no explanation. Just the prompt — 3 to 5 sentences ending in one open question.

##### Mode B — Generative when warmup_anchor is null

Fallback mode for the first run before the pacing guide is imported, or for lesson dates that fall outside all pacing guide date ranges. Flow 3 generates from the lesson context and student profile alone. Output is valid but less pedagogically grounded than Mode A.

###### System Prompt — Mode B (Generative)

Use when warmup_anchor is null in lesson_context_snapshot

You are generating a warm-up prompt for a high school CTE student in a Sports, Entertainment, and Event Management/Marketing course.

YOUR ROLE:
You write cognitive entry points — not review questions, not preview questions. A cognitive entry point makes a student think before the lesson begins, creates productive tension that the lesson resolves, and is interesting enough that a student who doesn't have to engage probably will anyway. It never feels like homework.

TODAY'S LESSON:
Course: {course_name}
Objective: {objective}
Activity: {activity}
Key vocabulary: {vocabulary}
Connection to prior lesson: {prior_connection}
Competencies addressed: {competency_texts_formatted}

STUDENT CONTEXT:
Name: {first_name}
Competencies this student has already covered this term: {competencies_addressed_count} of {total_competencies}
New competency areas introduced today: {competency_gaps_formatted}

Evaluation history (last 3 assignments):
{evaluation_signals_formatted}

Warm-up engagement history:
Average engagement score: {avg_engagement_score} / 3.0
Extra credit replies submitted: {extra_credit_count}

ARCHETYPE SELECTED: {archetype}

Archetype definitions:
PARADOX — Surface a genuine contradiction. Two things both seem true but can't both be right without nuance the lesson provides. Student is conceptually strong, struggles with application.
CONCRETE SCENARIO — Present a specific real-sounding industry situation requiring judgment. Abstraction emerges from the scenario. Student works well with tangible problems.
BRIDGE — Connect today's new competency domain to something the student has already covered. Activate prior knowledge. Reduce friction of new material.
PROVOCATION — A genuine intellectual challenge with no easy answer. No scaffolding. Rewards prior knowledge. For students who are strong across the board.

WRITING CONSTRAINTS — follow all of these without exception:
1. 3 to 5 sentences maximum. Not a word more.
2. Never use a list, bullet point, or numbered items.
3. Never use fill-in-the-blank format.
4. Never ask more than one question. The prompt ends with exactly one question.
5. The question must be open-ended — no yes/no questions.
6. Never mention grades, points, scores, or evaluation.
7. Never use the words "today" or "lesson" — the student already knows what day it is.
8. Never reference the student's past performance explicitly — use it to inform the angle, not the content.
9. Write in second person ("you", "your") — address the student directly.
10. Industry-grounded — the scenario or example must be real or plausible in sports, entertainment, or event contexts. No generic business examples.
11. Vocabulary words may appear naturally but must not be defined — the prompt assumes the student will encounter the definition in class.

OUTPUT FORMAT:
Return only the warm-up prompt text. No preamble, no label, no explanation, no quotation marks around the output. Just the prompt itself — 3 to 5 sentences ending in one open question.

◈
Placeholder population logic in Flow 3: Flow 3 parses both JSON snapshots from the queue row. Mode A or B is selected based on whether warmup_anchor is present and non-null. As of July 2026, PacingGuide_CAS_Context_v2.json is the authoritative source — warm-up anchors are now the full teacher-authored lesson card prompts, not compressed summaries. The student_profile_snapshot now includes SOL subject-area signals via sol_correlations on each competency rubric. Flow 3 can use SOL subject area as a tertiary personalization signal: a student whose shadow matrix shows consistently low engagement on History & Social Sciences-correlated competencies is a different framing target than one who is strong there. This is not yet wired into the Mode A or B system prompts — it is available data pending a future system prompt update. The archetype is selected using the shadow matrix + decision table (see Archetypes section). {shadow_archetype_note} in Mode A is a plain-text line from the shadow matrix: "Shadow matrix suggests PARADOX (72% confidence, 5 warm-ups, rising trend)." This gives Gemini context for why the archetype was selected, which improves adaptation quality at the margin.

◈
Constraint 10 in Mode A is the key discipline: "If the anchor already perfectly fits the archetype and student — return it unchanged." Flow 3 must not adapt for the sake of adapting. When the anchor is already a PARADOX and the selected archetype is PARADOX and the student's history doesn't change the framing, the right output is the anchor verbatim. Unnecessary adaptation degrades the teacher's intended prompt. The test: would a teacher reading both versions prefer the adaptation? If not, return the anchor.

◈
Constraint 8 is unchanged across both modes. The prompt must never say "you've been struggling with application" or "since you're good at analysis." Students will read these docs. Using evaluation history to inform the angle is personalization. Making the history explicit in the text is surveillance. The distinction is everything — the archetype and shadow matrix handle the former, the constraint enforces the latter.

Document format

#### What Flow 3 creates

Flow 3 creates one Google Doc per student per class day. The doc has a fixed three-zone structure. Zone delimiters are exact strings — Script 25 uses them to extract the response zone and detect extra credit replies. They must not be altered.

Header

Doc title: Warm-Up [YYYY-MM-DD] — [Student First Name]

First line of doc body: Warm-Up — [Date in readable format] — [First Name]

Not a zone — just identification. Appears before Zone 1.

Zone 1 · Prompt

Opens with: ── WARM-UP PROMPT ──

Content: the generated warm-up text (3–5 sentences)

Closes with: ── END PROMPT ──

Font: 12pt, color #333333. Read-only intent — student does not write here.

Zone 2 · Response

Opens with: ── YOUR RESPONSE ──

Content: blank — student writes here during the warm-up period

Script 25 extracts everything after this marker (up to the FEEDBACK marker if present)

Font: 11pt, color #202124. Student writes in default style.

Zone 3 · Feedback

Written by Script 25 after Flow 4 evaluates the response — not by Flow 3.

Opens with: ── FEEDBACK ──

Content: Flow 4's pedagogical feedback (1–3 sentences, no scores mentioned)

Closes with: ── END FEEDBACK ──

Any student text after ── END FEEDBACK ── = extra credit reply, detected next night.

◈
Drive folder structure Flow 3 must create:
[Admin Root Folder] / [Subject] / [Teacher Name] / [Period N] / Warm-Ups / [Student Name] /
Flow 3 resolves this path using the lesson context snapshot (course_name, period) and the teacher's admin root folder ID from the queue row's embedded context. The student subfolder is created if it doesn't exist — same getOrCreate pattern as Module 1's resolveFolder_(). Doc is shared with the student as editor via google_id from the queue row.

Studio setup

#### Flow 3 configuration

How to configure this flow in Google Workspace Studio. Settings that differ from Module 1's Flow 2 are marked.

Setting | Value | Notes |

Flow name | CAS — Warm-Up Generation | Matches naming convention of Flows 1 and 2 |

Trigger | Sheets — row added/updated | Spreadsheet: Central Ledger · Sheet: WarmUpQueue · Condition: Status = PENDING |

Model | Gemini Pro | Same model as Flow 2 |

Temperature | 0.7 | Higher than Flow 2 (0.3) — warm-ups need creative variation |

Max tokens | 400 | 3–5 sentences is approximately 60–100 tokens. 400 gives headroom. |

System prompt | Mode A (anchor) or Mode B (generative) | Mode selected based on warmup_anchor presence in snapshot. See System Prompt section above for both full texts. |

Shadow matrix | Read from student_profile_snapshot | cross_confidence ≥ 0.75 overrides decision table archetype selection. Included as {shadow_archetype_note} in Mode A prompt. |

Archetype write-back | WarmUpQueue col 19 (archetype) | Flow 3 writes the selected archetype back to the queue row after selection. Script 23 reads this nightly for shadow matrix updates. |

Input connector | Sheets — read row | Read lesson_context_snapshot and student_profile_snapshot from trigger row |

Output connector 1 | Drive — create file | Create Google Doc in resolved folder path. Doc title: Warm-Up [date] — [first_name] |

Output connector 2 | Drive — share file | Share created doc with student email as editor |

Output connector 3 | Sheets — update row | Write doc_id, doc_url to trigger row. Set status = DELIVERED. |

Error handling | Write ERROR to status | On any connector failure: set WarmUpQueue status = ERROR. Script 24's dedup check prevents re-queuing. |

Concurrency | Per-teacher lane | One active execution per teacher at a time. Same Turnstile pattern as Module 1 if multiple teachers run simultaneously. |

◈
Archetype selection happens inside Flow 3's pre-processing step — before the Gemini call. Flow 3 reads the student profile snapshot, evaluates the four archetype conditions in priority order (PROVOCATION → PARADOX → CONCRETE SCENARIO → BRIDGE), selects the matching archetype, populates the {archetype} placeholder in the system prompt, then sends to Gemini. The archetype selection logic is implemented as a Flow 3 JavaScript transformation step, not by Gemini itself.

◈
Admin root folder ID must be embedded in the queue row. Flow 3 needs the admin root folder ID to resolve the drive folder path. Script 24 should embed cfg.adminRootFolderId in the lesson_context_snapshot JSON when building queue rows. Add "admin_root_folder_id": cfg.adminRootFolderId to the snapshot object in Script 24's lessonContextSnapshot construction. This is the one config value not already present in the snapshot — add it before first deployment.

Studio Flow 4

#### Warm-Up Evaluation

Flow 4 is simpler than Flow 3 — it evaluates a response against two criteria and returns structured JSON. No doc creation, no Drive operations. Pure inference.

Setting | Value | Notes |

Flow name | CAS — Warm-Up Evaluation | |

Trigger | Sheets — row updated | Central Ledger · WarmUpQueue · Status = PENDING_EVAL |

Model | Gemini Pro | |

Temperature | 0.2 | Low — evaluation needs consistency not creativity |

Max tokens | 256 | JSON response is small. Strict cap prevents prose leakage. |

Input | response_text + lesson_context_snapshot from trigger row | Flow 4 reconstructs the original prompt from the snapshot for context |

Output connector | Sheets — update row | Write grammar_score, engagement_score, flow4_feedback to trigger row. Set status = SCORED. |

###### System Prompt — Studio Flow 4

Configure in: Flow settings → System instructions

You are evaluating a high school CTE student's warm-up response in a Sports, Entertainment, and Event Management/Marketing course.

THE WARM-UP PROMPT WAS:
{original_prompt_text}

THE STUDENT'S RESPONSE:
{response_text}

WORD COUNT SCORE (already computed — do not re-evaluate):
{word_count_score} / 6 points

YOUR TASK:
Evaluate the response on exactly two criteria:

1. GRAMMAR AND SENTENCE STRUCTURE — 0 or 1 point
Award 1 point if the response consists of cohesive sentences with no errors that significantly impede comprehension.
Award 0 points if errors significantly impede comprehension.
Minor errors (comma splices, informal register) do not reduce the score.
Context: this is a verbal warm-up written in class, not a formal essay.

2. ENGAGEMENT — 0, 1, 2, or 3 points
3 = Genuine. Directly addresses the prompt with original thought. Shows the student actually considered the question.
2 = Surface. On-topic but formulaic, thin, or restates the question without extending it.
1 = Minimal. Tangentially related, or so brief that genuine engagement cannot be confirmed.
0 = Off-topic or filler. Does not engage with the prompt at all.

FEEDBACK WRITING RULES — all mandatory:
- Write 1 to 3 sentences of pedagogical feedback.
- NEVER mention points, scores, grades, or evaluation criteria.
- NEVER say "good job", "well done", or similar praise without substance.
- NEVER say "you need to improve" or similar deficit framing.
- Write something that advances the student's thinking about today's topic.
- The student will read this before the next class. It should make them want to think more.
- If the response was genuine (score 3): push further. Ask the implicit next question.
- If the response was surface (score 2): make the depth visible. Show what a deeper answer would look at.
- If the response was minimal (score 1): find the thread of engagement and pull it.
- If the response was off-topic (score 0): write one sentence connecting what they wrote to the actual prompt.

OUTPUT FORMAT — strict:
Return ONLY valid JSON. No preamble, no markdown, no explanation outside the JSON.
{
"grammar": 0 or 1,
"engagement": 0, 1, 2, or 3,
"feedback": "your feedback text here"
}

◈
Even a score-0 response gets pedagogical feedback. A student who wrote something off-topic still sees one sentence when they open their doc before next class. The sentence finds whatever thread connects their response to the prompt and makes that connection visible. This is not remediation — it's an invitation. The system never tells a student they failed a warm-up. That's the teacher's conversation, not the system's.

Classroom Agency System · Studio Flow 3 + 4 Specification · v1.2 — Anchor-Aware + Shadow Matrix + Bridging Flow
Warm-Up Generation · Warm-Up Evaluation · Bridging · Four Archetypes · 11-Point Scoring

Studio Flow 5 — NEW

#### Bridging Flow

Generates a 2–3 sentence connector between the student's most recent warm-up response and today's lesson. Runs on its own PENDING_BRIDGE queue row, then promotes that row to PENDING — this two-status hand-off is what actually enforces "before Flow 3," not a compound trigger condition on Flow 5 (Script 24 only ever writes PENDING_BRIDGE for a row that already has a prior scored response, so the condition needs nothing else). Output written to bridge_output (WarmUpQueue col 21). Flow 3's own trigger condition never changes — it still only ever fires on Status = PENDING, whether a row arrived there directly or via Flow 5 — and prepends bridge_output to the warm-up doc when present. Strictly additive — no bridge is ever missing; students without prior responses simply start at PENDING and skip Flow 5 entirely.

Trigger: WarmUpQueue PENDING_BRIDGE
Temperature: 0.4
Max tokens: 150
Skips: no prior response

##### Configuration

Setting |
Value / Notes |

Name | CAS — Bridging Flow |

Trigger | WarmUpQueue · Status = PENDING_BRIDGE — no compound condition needed; Script 24 only writes this status when flow5_prior_response is already present in the snapshot |

Temperature | 0.4 — connective not generative; lower than Flow 3's 0.7 |

Max tokens | 150 — one paragraph, 2–3 sentences maximum |

Input | flow5_prior_response · pacing_prior_connection · course_name — all from snapshot |

Output | bridge_output (col 21) on WarmUpQueue row · Status set to PENDING — this is the hand-off to Flow 3, not a status that "stays" unchanged |

Run order | Before Flow 3 — Flow 3 reads bridge_output and prepends if present |

##### System prompt

System Prompt — Flow 5 Bridging
Flow settings → System instructions

You are writing a one-paragraph bridge for a high school CTE student in a Sports, Entertainment, and Event Management/Marketing course.

YOUR ROLE:
The student wrote a warm-up response in a previous class. Today's lesson builds on what they engaged with. Write 2-3 sentences that make that connection explicit - what they thought about before, how it leads to today, and why that matters. The bridge goes at the top of their warm-up doc before the new prompt.

WHAT THE STUDENT WROTE LAST TIME:
{flow5_prior_response}

HOW TODAY CONNECTS TO THE PRIOR LESSON:
{pacing_prior_connection}

TODAY'S COURSE:
{course_name}

CONSTRAINTS:
1. Exactly 2-3 sentences. No more, no less.
2. Address the student in second person ("you", "your").
3. Reference what they actually wrote - name a specific idea if they expressed one.
4. Do not preview today's lesson - only make the connection to the prior one.
5. Never mention grades, scores, or warm-ups.
6. Avoid "last time" - use "recently" or "in your last response" at most once.
7. End with a sentence that creates forward momentum.
8. If the prior response is too short or generic to reference, use only pacing_prior_connection.

OUTPUT FORMAT:
Return only the bridge paragraph. No label, no preamble. 2-3 sentences.

◈ Strictly additive. Flow 3 checks bridge_output before generating. If non-null, it prepends with a separator line. If null, the doc generates without it. No student ever sees a gap where a bridge should be.

◈ Constraint 3 is the discipline. Prior response is capped at 800 chars in the snapshot. "You connected ticket pricing to consumer income" is more useful than "you thought about industry factors." Constraint 8 provides graceful fallback when the prior response is too thin to name specifically.

### DeploymentGuide

Step-by-step instructions from script files to a running warm-up pipeline. Check each step as you complete it. Progress saves in your browser.

Phases

37
Steps

~4h
Estimated time

0
New vendors

Code 0/5

Data 0/4

Phase 1 · ~45 min

#### Add scripts to
the project

All new scripts bind to the Central Ledger spreadsheet — the same project as your existing Module 1 scripts. Script 07 (Teacher Dashboard) lives in its own separate project.

✓

Step 1 of 5

Open the Central Ledger spreadsheet → Extensions → Apps Script

This is the spreadsheet that has your Ledger, ReviewQueue, STAGING_PIPELINE, and TeacherMatrix tabs. The Apps Script project already has your Module 1 scripts (Scripts 02–06, 10, etc.).

✓

Step 2 of 5

Add nine new script files to the Central Ledger project

For each file below: click the + next to Files → Script → name it exactly as shown → paste the contents from the downloaded file.

- 22_LessonContextHandler — paste 22_LessonContextHandler.js

- 22b_CompetencyRegistryImporter — paste 22b_CompetencyRegistryImporter.js

- 23_StudentProfileManager — paste 23_StudentProfileManager.js

- 24_WarmUpBridge — paste 24_WarmUpBridge.js

- 25_WarmUpWriter — paste 25_WarmUpWriter.js

- 26_CompetencyAlignmentLog — paste 26_CompetencyAlignmentLog.js

- 29_PacingGuideManager — paste 29_PacingGuideManager.js

- 30_CompetencyRubricImporter — paste 30_CompetencyRubricImporter.js

- 31_ArtifactCompetencyBridge — paste 31_ArtifactCompetencyBridge.js

Save after each file (Ctrl+S / Cmd+S). Do not run anything yet.

✓

Step 3 of 5

Update Script 00 — add Module 2 tabs and config

Open 00_SharedConfig in the same project. Make two additions from 00_SharedConfig_M2_ADDENDUM_v2.js.

⚠ Already done in the current codebase — this addendum is merged into 00_SharedConfig.js's getConfig_() (the m2Enabled key is already present there). Nothing to copy for a fresh checkout; the two sub-steps below are a historical record of what the merge covered.

- Find the tabs: object in getConfig_(). Replace it with the complete version from Section A of the addendum file. It adds: lessonContext, competencyRegistry, alignmentLog, reportRegistry, studentProfiles, warmUpQueue, warmUpRegistry, classSchedule, pacingGuide, competencyRubrics.

- In the return {} block of getConfig_(), add the line from Section B:
Add to getConfig_() return objectApps Script
m2Enabled: p.M2_ENABLED || "false",
shadowMatrixConfidenceThreshold: 0.75,
shadowMatrixDecayFactor: 0.85,

Also update Script 00 in the Script 07 (Teacher Dashboard) project with the same Section A tabs update.

✓

Step 4 of 5

Update Script 07 — replace with new Teacher Dashboard file

Open the Script 07 project (the standalone Teacher Dashboard web app). Replace the contents of the main script file with 07_TeacherDashboard.js. Do not redeploy yet — that happens in Phase 3.

◈
Script 07 is a separate project from the Central Ledger. It has its own Apps Script editor. Open the Teacher Dashboard spreadsheet (or web app URL) → Extensions → Apps Script to find it.

✓

Step 5 of 5

Set M2_ENABLED in Script Properties

In the Central Ledger Apps Script project: Project Settings (gear icon) → Script Properties → Add property.

Property | Value |

M2_ENABLED | true |

All other M2 properties are set automatically by the scripts during setup. Only M2_ENABLED requires manual entry.

Phase 2 · ~10 min

#### Upload data files
to Drive

Three JSON/CSV files need to be in your teacher Drive folder before the import scripts can run. Two were produced in this session. One you already have. Use PacingGuide_CAS_Context_v2.json — not the original. CompetencyRubrics.json now includes SOL correlations for 197 of 221 competencies (sourced from both VDOE official printable versions, including the 8177 file discovered embedded in the Canvas management cartridge).

✓

Step 1 of 4

Upload CompetencyRegistry.csv to your teacher Drive folder

Download CompetencyRegistry.csv from this session. Upload it to your teacher assignments folder in Drive — the same folder that was created during Module 1 teacher setup (visible in your manual doc under "Your Admin Folder").

◈
File must be named exactly: CompetencyRegistry.csv — the importer searches by this exact filename. 221 rows expected (113 × 8175, 108 × 8177).

✓

Step 2 of 4

Upload CompetencyRubrics.json to your teacher Drive folder

Download CompetencyRubrics.json from this session. Upload to the same teacher folder. 221 rubrics — one per competency across both courses. Each rubric has demonstration indicators and skill questions pre-mapped to the four warm-up archetypes.

◈
File must be named exactly: CompetencyRubrics.json. If Drive renames it on upload, rename it back before running the importer.

✓

Step 3 of 4

Confirm PacingGuide_CAS_Context_v2.json is in your teacher Drive folder

This file was uploaded to Drive in a previous session. Confirm it's still in your teacher folder. If not, re-upload it now.

◈
File must be named exactly: PacingGuide_CAS_Context.json. Contains 20 units covering August 2026 – June 2027 with warmup anchors, competency IDs, and prior lesson connections per unit.

✓

Step 4 of 4

Confirm all three files are in the same folder

Open your teacher Drive folder and confirm all three files are present before proceeding.

- CompetencyRegistry.csv

- CompetencyRubrics.json

- PacingGuide_CAS_Context.json

Phase 3 · ~60 min

#### Run setup functions
& configure tabs

Run functions from the Script Editor in the order shown. Each function is listed with its script file. Run them one at a time — not as triggers.

✓

Step 1 of 14

Run createModule2Tabs_() from Script 22

Creates the four lightweight tabs: LessonContext, CompetencyRegistry, AlignmentLog, ReportRegistry. Select 22_LessonContextHandler in the file dropdown, choose createModule2Tabs_ in the function dropdown, click Run.

Safe to re-run — skips existing tabs. Expected output in logs: "M2 tabs created/verified."

✓

Step 2 of 14

Run importCompetencyRegistry() from Script 22b

Imports CompetencyRegistry.csv from your teacher folder into the CompetencyRegistry tab. After running, open the tab and confirm 221 rows (plus header).

Then run validateRegistryImport() to confirm:

- Total rows: 221

- Sports Entertainment and Event Marketing (8175): 113

- Sports Entertainment and Event Management (8177): 108

- Issues found: none

If the file isn't found, confirm it's named exactly CompetencyRegistry.csv in your teacher folder.

✓

Step 3 of 14

Run importPacingGuide() from Script 29

Imports PacingGuide_CAS_Context.json into the PacingGuide tab. Creates the tab if it doesn't exist. Invalidates the Script Properties cache.

Then run validatePacingGuide() — confirm:

- Total units: 20

- Missing anchor: 0

- Missing start/end date: 0

- All units valid ✓

✓

Step 4 of 14

Run importCompetencyRubrics() from Script 30

Imports CompetencyRubrics.json into the CompetencyRubrics tab. Creates the tab if it doesn't exist. Calls validateRubricImport() automatically after writing.

Confirm in logs:

- Total rows: 221

- Course 8175: 113 rubrics

- Course 8177: 108 rubrics

- Missing questions: NONE ✓

- Missing indicators: NONE ✓

✓

Step 5 of 14

Run createWarmUpTabs_() from Script 23

Creates six warm-up tabs: StudentProfiles, WarmUpQueue, WarmUpRegistry, ClassSchedule, PacingGuide (if not already created by Step 3), CompetencyRubrics (if not already created by Step 4). Safe to re-run.

Confirm in spreadsheet: all six tabs exist with correct headers. WarmUpQueue should have 21 columns. StudentProfiles should have 14.

✓

Step 6 of 14

Populate the ClassSchedule tab manually

Open the ClassSchedule tab on the Central Ledger. Add one row per period you teach. The header row is already set.

teacher_email | period | day_type | course_name | active |

your@email.com | 1 | DAILY | Sports Entertainment and Event Management | TRUE |

your@email.com | 2 | ODD | Sports Entertainment and Event Marketing | TRUE |

your@email.com | 3 | EVEN | Sports Entertainment and Event Management | TRUE |

your@email.com | 4 | ODD | Sports Entertainment and Event Marketing | TRUE |

day_type values: DAILY (every school day), ODD (1st, 3rd, 5th... of month), EVEN (2nd, 4th, 6th...). Course names must match the subject column in CompetencyRegistry exactly.

✓

Step 7 of 14

Run addCompetencyIdsColumn_() from Script 31

Adds a competency_ids column to your Teacher Matrix spreadsheet. Run this once — it checks for the existing column and skips if already present.

After running, open your Teacher Matrix spreadsheet and confirm column 16 is labeled competency_ids. The DraftUnits sheet gets the same column.

✓

Step 8 of 14

Tag existing Teacher Matrix assignments with competency IDs

Open your Teacher Matrix. In the new competency_ids column (column 16), enter comma-separated competency IDs for each assignment that addresses specific VDOE competencies.

Format: 8177-47,8177-52,8177-53

Competency IDs follow the pattern COURSE-TASKNUMBER. Find them in your CompetencyRegistry tab. Column B shows the competency text, column A shows the ID.

◈
This can be done gradually. It doesn't block warm-up generation. Start with your current active assignments and add past ones over the first few weeks. Script 31 only processes COMPLETE artifacts — partial tagging still produces partial per-student coverage.

✓

Step 9 of 14

Run installWarmUpTriggers_() from Script 23

Installs three nightly time-based triggers. Run from the Script Editor — select Script 23, function installWarmUpTriggers_, click Run.

Function | Time |

updateAllStudentProfiles | Every day at 3:00am |

runWarmUpEvaluation | Every day at 3:15am |

buildWarmUpQueues | Every day at 3:30am |

Verify: Triggers panel (clock icon in Script Editor sidebar) → confirm three triggers appear. Google will ask for authorization — click Allow.

✓

Step 10 of 14

Run installRegistrationTrigger_() from Script 25

Installs the every-5-minute safety net trigger that registers delivered warm-ups and catches any that were delivered after the main evaluation window.

Function: registerDeliveredWarmUps · Every 5 minutes. Confirm in Triggers panel.

✓

Step 11 of 14

Run installArtifactSyncTrigger_() from Script 31

Installs the 3:05am artifact competency sync trigger — runs between Script 23 (3:00am) and Script 25 (3:15am) to overlay per-student competency coverage from completed work artifacts.

Function: syncArtifactCompetencies · Every day at 3:05am. Confirm in Triggers panel. Full sequence: 3:00am S23 → 3:05am S31 → 3:15am S25 → 3:30am S24.

✓

Step 12 of 14

Run installModule2Triggers_() from Script 22

Installs the alignment backfill safety trigger — runs every 5 minutes to catch any lesson context submissions that weren't aligned in real time.

Function: runAlignmentLogBackfill_ · Every 5 minutes.

✓

Step 13 of 14

Redeploy Script 07 — Teacher Dashboard

The new Script 07 adds the warm-up readiness panel and the "New Lesson" modal. Redeploy the existing web app — the URL does not change.

- Open the Script 07 project → Deploy → Manage deployments

- Select your existing deployment → click the Edit pencil icon

- Version: New version

- Click Deploy

The dashboard URL stays the same. Teachers do not need a new link. The "New Lesson" button and the readiness panel will appear on next refresh.

✓

Step 14 of 14

Verify trigger count in the Triggers panel

Open Script Editor → Triggers (clock icon). Confirm all five triggers are present.

Function | Schedule | Script |

updateAllStudentProfiles | Daily 3:00am | S23 |

syncArtifactCompetencies | Daily 3:05am | S31 |

runWarmUpEvaluation | Daily 3:15am | S25 |

buildWarmUpQueues | Daily 3:30am | S24 |

registerDeliveredWarmUps | Every 5 min | S25 |

runAlignmentLogBackfill_ | Every 5 min | S22 |

Phase 4 · ~2–3 hours

#### Configure
Studio Flows

Three flows in Google Workspace Studio. Keep CAS_Flow3_Flow4_Specification.html open in another tab — it has the exact system prompts, temperatures, and field mappings for all three flows.

✓

Step 1 of 6

Open Google Workspace Studio and create Flow 5 — Bridging Flow

Create this flow first because Flow 3 depends on it running before generation. Flow 5 is the simplest — temperature 0.4, max 150 tokens, one input field, one output field.

Setting | Value |

Name | CAS — Bridging Flow |

Trigger | WarmUpQueue · Status = PENDING_BRIDGE — no compound condition needed; Script 24 only writes this status when a prior scored response already exists |

Temperature | 0.4 |

Max tokens | 150 |

Input | flow5_prior_response · pacing_prior_connection · course_name from lesson_context_snapshot |

Output | Write to bridge_output (col 21) · Set status = PENDING |

System prompt | See CAS_Flow3_Flow4_Specification.html → Flow 5 section |

Runs on its own PENDING_BRIDGE row, then promotes that row to PENDING — this hand-off is what actually enforces "before Flow 3," not a compound trigger condition on Flow 5. A student with no prior response never reaches PENDING_BRIDGE at all; their row starts at PENDING directly and skips Flow 5 entirely.

✓

Step 2 of 6

Create Flow 3 — Warm-Up Generation (Mode A: anchor-aware)

The primary generation flow. Runs after Flow 5. Checks for warmup_anchor in the snapshot — if present, personalizes it (Mode A). If null, generates from scratch (Mode B).

Setting | Value |

Name | CAS — Warm-Up Generation |

Trigger | WarmUpQueue · Status = PENDING — unchanged either way a row gets here: directly from Script 24 (no prior response) or via Flow 5 promoting a PENDING_BRIDGE row (returning student) |

Temperature | 0.7 |

Max tokens | 400 |

Input | lesson_context_snapshot + student_profile_snapshot from queue row |

Output 1 | Create Google Doc in student warm-up folder (path from admin_root_folder_id in snapshot) |

Output 2 | Share doc with student_email as editor |

Output 3 | Write doc_id + doc_url to queue row · Write selected archetype to col 19 · Set status = DELIVERED |

System prompt | See CAS_Flow3_Flow4_Specification.html → Mode A and Mode B prompts |

The doc structure: bridge paragraph (if bridge_output present) → separator → warm-up prompt → response line → student response zone.

✓

Step 3 of 6

Create Flow 4 — Warm-Up Evaluation

Evaluates student responses after Script 25 extracts the text and queues the row for AI scoring. Returns grammar and engagement scores plus brief pedagogical feedback.

Setting | Value |

Name | CAS — Warm-Up Evaluation |

Trigger | WarmUpQueue · Status = PENDING_EVAL |

Temperature | 0.2 |

Max tokens | 256 |

Input | response_text + lesson_context_snapshot from queue row |

Output | Write grammar_score + engagement_score + flow4_feedback · Set status = SCORED |

Response format | JSON only — no preamble, no markdown: {"grammar":0|1,"engagement":0|1|2|3,"feedback":"..."} |

System prompt | See CAS_Flow3_Flow4_Specification.html → Flow 4 section |

Feedback tone: forward-looking, never punitive. Never mentions scores. Written as a question or observation the student can act on.

✓

Step 4 of 6

Test Flow 5 with a synthetic queue row

Create a test row in WarmUpQueue with status = PENDING_BRIDGE (not PENDING — that's Flow 3's trigger, and Flow 5 no longer watches it) and a non-null flow5_prior_response in the lesson context snapshot JSON. Trigger Flow 5 and confirm bridge_output (col 21) is populated with 2–3 sentences and status has been set to PENDING.

✓

Step 5 of 6

Test Flow 3 with a synthetic queue row

Create a test row with status = PENDING, a populated lesson_context_snapshot (include a warmup_anchor to test Mode A). Trigger Flow 3. Confirm: warm-up doc appears in student Drive folder, doc is shared, doc_id written to queue row, status = DELIVERED, archetype written to col 19.

✓

Step 6 of 6

Test Flow 4 with a synthetic queue row

Write 35+ words of student text into the test doc. Run runWarmUpEvaluation() from Script 25 manually (Script Editor → select Script 25 → run). This extracts the response and sets status = PENDING_EVAL. Then trigger Flow 4. Confirm: grammar_score + engagement_score populated, feedback written, status = SCORED.

Phase 5 · ~30 min

#### Smoke test
the full pipeline

Run the complete pipeline manually end-to-end before the first overnight cron. This catches any integration issues before real student data is involved. Do this the day before classes start.

✓

Step 1 of 8

Log a lesson via the Teacher Dashboard

Open your Teacher Dashboard URL. Click New Lesson in the header. Fill in: lesson date (tomorrow's date), learning objective, activity description, 2–3 competencies from one of your courses. Click Log lesson.

Confirm immediately:

- Toast: "Lesson logged. Alignment will be recorded automatically."

- LessonContext tab: one new row, status = ALIGNMENT_LOGGED

- AlignmentLog tab: one row per competency selected

✓

Step 2 of 8

Run updateAllStudentProfiles() from Script 23

Simulates the 3:00am Stage 1 cron. Builds StudentProfile rows for all enrolled students. After running, open StudentProfiles tab — one row per student, with evaluation signals and warmup_scores populated from Module 1 history.

Students with no Module 1 history get empty evaluation_signals — correct. Shadow matrix will be empty {} until the first scored warm-up night.

✓

Step 3 of 8

Run syncArtifactCompetencies() from Script 31

Simulates the 3:05am artifact sync. Reads STAGING_PIPELINE for COMPLETE assignments, joins to Teacher Matrix competency_ids, updates StudentProfiles. If no assignments are tagged yet, logs "No competency tags found" — expected.

✓

Step 4 of 8

Run buildWarmUpQueues() from Script 24

Simulates the 3:30am Stage 3 cron. Reads the lesson you logged in Step 1 (tomorrow's date), determines which periods meet tomorrow by date parity, and builds one WarmUpQueue row per enrolled student in those periods.

Confirm in WarmUpQueue tab:

- One row per student in the period(s) meeting tomorrow

- Status = PENDING for a student with no prior scored warm-up, or PENDING_BRIDGE for a returning student with one — on a fresh deployment with no warm-up history yet, expect every row to be PENDING

- lesson_context_snapshot is populated JSON (not empty)

- student_profile_snapshot is populated JSON

- Snapshot contains warmup_anchor if pacing guide was imported

- Snapshot contains competency_rubrics array

✓

Step 5 of 8

Trigger Flow 5 (if any PENDING_BRIDGE rows exist) and Flow 3

In Studio, if step 4 produced any PENDING_BRIDGE rows, manually trigger Flow 5 on one of them first — its own final step promotes that row to PENDING once bridge_output is written. On a fresh deployment with no warm-up history, skip straight to Flow 3 below; every row is already PENDING. Then trigger Flow 3 on a PENDING row.

Confirm:

- Flow 5 (if triggered): bridge_output (col 21) populated with 2–3 sentences, status set to PENDING

- Flow 3: warm-up doc created in student Drive folder

- Flow 3: doc_id + doc_url written to queue row

- Flow 3: archetype (col 19) populated

- Flow 3: status = DELIVERED

- WarmUpRegistry: one new row

✓

Step 6 of 8

Write a test response and run evaluation

Open one of the warm-up docs that was created. Write 35+ words below the response line. Then run runWarmUpEvaluation() from Script 25 in the Script Editor.

Confirm:

- WarmUpQueue row: response_text extracted, word_count populated, status = PENDING_EVAL

- After Flow 4 runs: grammar_score + engagement_score populated, status = SCORED

- Warm-up doc: feedback written below the student's response

- WarmUpRegistry row: total_score populated

✓

Step 7 of 8

Generate alignment and warm-up reports

Run both report generators from the Script Editor to confirm end-of-term reporting works.

- Run generateAlignmentReport() from Script 26 → confirm a Google Doc is created in your teacher folder, ReportRegistry row written

- Run generateWarmUpReport() from Script 25 → confirm a grade report Doc is created, ReportRegistry row written (type: WARMUP_TERM)

✓

Step 8 of 8

Confirm nightly cron is ready

The system is ready to run autonomously. Tonight's cron will be the first live run. You don't need to do anything.

Before logging off:

- Log tomorrow's actual lesson via the Teacher Dashboard

- Confirm ClassSchedule has the correct day_type for tomorrow's calendar date

◈
The system takes over from here. At 3:00am scripts run, at 6:00am flows generate, students open their docs in first period and their warm-up is waiting. The only thing you need to do each day is log the next day's lesson before you leave school. Everything else is automatic.

##### 🎉 Deployment complete

All 37 steps complete. The warm-up pipeline runs tonight at 3am.
Tomorrow your students open their docs and their warm-up is waiting.

CAS Platform

Module 2 · Lightweight · Data Schema

Overview
LessonContext
CompetencyRegistry
AlignmentLog
Notes
Full Build Tabs

Data Schema Reference

### Module 2 Lightweight
Tab Definitions

3 New Tabs
Scripts 22 + 26
No Studio Flows
Additive — no M1 changes

#### Tab Overview

Three new tabs added to the existing Central Ledger spreadsheet. No existing tabs are modified.

##### LessonContext

One row per lesson submission. Written by Script 22 when the teacher submits the Lesson Context Form. The source of truth for what was taught, when, and why.

18 columns
Written by: S22
Read by: S26, S27

##### CompetencyRegistry

One row per competency from the district framework. Set up once. Never written by any script after initial load — only read. Powers the form dropdown and the alignment log.

7 columns
Written by: Manual / S28
Read by: S22, S26

##### AlignmentLog

Append-only. One row per lesson-competency pair — so one lesson with three competencies produces three rows. Accumulates automatically. Feeds end-of-term documentation export.

9 columns
Written by: S26
Read by: dashboard, export

##### No other tabs

The lightweight build does not create WarmUpQueue, StudentProfiles, or ConceptMastery — those belong to the full Module 2 build. Module 1 tabs (ReviewQueue, STAGING_PIPELINE, etc.) are untouched.

M1 tabs: read-only from M2 perspective

◈
Naming convention: Tab names use PascalCase to match the Module 1 pattern (ReviewQueue, STAGING_PIPELINE, etc.). The exception is STAGING_PIPELINE, which is all-caps to signal infrastructure. New tabs follow the mixed-case convention of operational tabs.

LessonContext

Central Ledger · one row per lesson

Written by Script 22 on every Lesson Context Form submission. One row = one lesson = one day (or one multi-day unit, at teacher discretion). The row is never deleted — updated in place as status progresses from RECEIVED → ALIGNMENT_LOGGED → FRAME_GENERATED (or SUPERSEDED/ERROR at any point). PUBLISHED, mentioned in earlier drafts of this lifecycle, has no described or built mechanism anywhere in this repo — left undocumented rather than invented.

ColumnTypeWriterDescription

── IDENTITY ──

lesson_id
String
S22
Auto-generated UUID. Format: LES-{YYYYMMDD}-{6hex} (widened from 4 to 6 hex chars — the original 65,536-value/day space had a real collision risk against WarmUpRegistry's queue_id lookup map). Primary key. Used in AlignmentLog foreign key.

teacher_email
String
S22
Authenticated submitter email. Pulled from Session.getActiveUser() at form submit — not a form field. Used for routing to teacher-specific dashboard view.

submitted_at
DateTime
S22
Server timestamp at row write. ISO 8601. Distinct from lesson_date — a teacher may submit Friday's lesson plan on Thursday evening.

── LESSON CONTENT (from form) ──

lesson_date
Date
S22
The date this lesson is taught. Form field — teacher-entered. YYYY-MM-DD. Used as display label in all downstream views and the alignment log.

period_or_class
String
S22
Optional. Period name or class identifier (e.g. "Period 3", "AP Lang"). Allows a teacher with multiple sections to submit separate lesson contexts for the same date. Nullable — single-section teachers leave blank.

activity_description
Text
S22
What students are doing today. Plain prose. Free-form. Not processed by any script until frame generation — consumed as-is by S27 (Lesson Frame Generator) into the "Today's Activity" section of the Lesson Frame Document.

learning_objective
Text
S22
What students can do by end of lesson that they couldn't at the start. Teacher's own language. Becomes the anchor for the Lesson Frame Document and the AlignmentLog objective field.

key_vocabulary
Text
S22
Comma-separated terms. E.g. "irony, dramatic irony, situational irony". S22 trims whitespace but does not validate against any vocabulary list. Stored as-written.

prior_lesson_connection
Text
S22
What last lesson covered that this one builds on. Free-form prose. Nullable — first lesson of a unit may leave blank. Used in Lesson Frame Document to make the learning progression explicit.

competency_ids
Text
S22
Comma-separated competency IDs from CompetencyRegistry. E.g. "ELA-W-1,ELA-W-3". S22 validates each ID exists in CompetencyRegistry before writing — unknown IDs trigger a validation error returned to the form.

◈
Correction to an earlier correction: a prior pass through this table claimed warm_up_generated "does not exist in the built schema and never has." That was wrong — it does exist, as a plain String status column (not the Boolean this document briefly described), created by 24_WarmUpBridge.js's createLessonContextWarmUpColumn_() and set to "DELIVERED" by 25_WarmUpWriter.js's markLessonContextDelivered_(). Found during the Script 27 build's own aftermath: its column index (14) was reused by this document's earlier frame_doc_id assignment, a real collision fixed in code by moving the Lesson Frame columns to 15-17 — see the row below and HISTORY.md.

warm_up_generated
String
S24
Status of warm-up generation for this lesson: empty (not yet queued), "QUEUED" (Script 24 has written WarmUpQueue rows for it), or "DELIVERED" (Studio Flow 3 has created all warm-up docs for this lesson). Full-build-only — never set on a lightweight-only installation. Added by createLessonContextWarmUpColumn_() (manual, once) or automatically on a fresh install's initial tab creation.

── LESSON FRAME (S27) ──

frame_doc_id
String
S27
Google Drive file ID of the generated Lesson Frame Document. Self-healing column — added to the header row on first use by any deployment predating this feature. Null until FRAME_GENERATED.

frame_doc_url
String
S27
Shareable URL for the Lesson Frame Document. This is the value S22 returns as frameDocUrl to the teacher dashboard, which opens it via the client's existing (previously dormant) window.open() hook. Null until FRAME_GENERATED.

frame_generated_at
DateTime
S27
Timestamp when Script 27 successfully created the Lesson Frame Document. Null until processed.

── STATUS + PIPELINE ──

status
Enum
S22, S26
Lifecycle state of this lesson row. See enum values below.

alignment_logged_at
DateTime
S26
Timestamp when Script 26 successfully wrote AlignmentLog rows for this lesson. Null until processed. S26 uses this as its processed check — it skips rows where this field is populated.

error_notes
Text
S22, S26
Human-readable error detail written by any script on validation failure. Null on success. Visible in admin health check. Never shown to students.

term
String
S22
Academic term this lesson belongs to, e.g. "2025-26 S2". Read from the CURRENT_TERM Script Property at submit time. Written once, never updated — a mid-term property change does not retroactively relabel existing rows. Last of the original 14 base columns; the columns above it (warm_up_generated, frame_doc_id/url/generated_at) were all appended after.

status enum

RECEIVED→ S22 has written the row. S26 has not yet run.

ALIGNMENT_LOGGED→ S26 has written AlignmentLog rows for this lesson.

FRAME_GENERATED→ S27 has created the Lesson Frame Document.

SUPERSEDED→ Teacher resubmitted the same lesson (same teacher_email + lesson_date + period_or_class); a new row exists with a new lesson_id. S26 skips SUPERSEDED rows.

ERROR→ A script encountered a failure. See error_notes. Retryable. The S22 backfill sets this when S26 has failed 6 times in a row, or once when retrying can't help (no competency_ids, row missing). Fix the row and set status back to RECEIVED to retry.

◈
Why ALIGNMENT_LOGGED not COMPLETE? The lightweight build has two distinct downstream steps: alignment logging (S26) and frame generation (S27) — both built, called back-to-back synchronously inside onLessonContextSubmit_(). Using separate status values still means either step's failure is independently visible: the status column tells you exactly where each row got to in the pipeline, and a row can rest at ALIGNMENT_LOGGED if frame generation itself failed non-fatally (see error_notes).

CompetencyRegistry

Central Ledger · one row per competency · set up once

The source of truth for the competency framework. Set up once from the district framework at the start of the year. Never modified by operational scripts — only read. A teacher with multiple courses may have multiple subjects in the same registry, filtered by the subject and teacher_email columns.

ColumnTypeWriterDescription

competency_id
String
Manual / S28
Primary key. Short human-readable code. E.g. ELA-W-1, SCI-INQ-3. No spaces. Validated against this registry when LessonContext form is submitted. Must be unique within the sheet.

competency_text
Text
Manual / S28
Full competency statement as written in the district framework. Pasted verbatim. This is the text that appears in the Alignment Log and any documentation exports.

subject
String
Manual / S28
Subject area. E.g. ELA, Science, History. Used to filter the Lesson Context Form dropdown to show only relevant competencies to each teacher.

grade_band
String
Manual / S28
Grade range. E.g. 9-10, 11-12. Nullable for frameworks that span all grades. Secondary filter for form dropdown.

strand
String
Manual / S28
Competency category within subject. E.g. Writing, Reading, Speaking & Listening. Used in AlignmentLog coverage report to group competencies by domain.

teacher_email
String
Manual / S28
Optional. If populated, this competency is only surfaced to this teacher's Lesson Context Form dropdown. Null = available to all teachers with matching subject. Enables subject-specific frameworks per teacher without separate sheets.

active
Boolean
Manual
Default TRUE. Set to FALSE to retire a competency without deleting it. Inactive competencies are excluded from the form dropdown but remain in AlignmentLog history. Preserves referential integrity.

◈
teacher_email is the lightweight alternative to a separate sheet per teacher. If you later run Module 2 for colleagues, their competency frameworks can coexist in the same registry tab, filtered by their email. No schema change required.

AlignmentLog

Central Ledger · one row per lesson-competency pair · append-only

Script 26 writes one row here for every competency ID in a LessonContext row. A lesson with three competencies = three AlignmentLog rows, all sharing the same lesson_id foreign key. Append-only — rows are never updated or deleted. This is the artifact that becomes term-end documentation.

ColumnTypeWriterDescription

── IDENTITY ──

log_id
String
S26
Auto-generated UUID for this specific log row. Format: ALG-{YYYYMMDD}-{4hex}. Not the lesson ID. Used if rows ever need to be referenced individually (e.g. in a future export function).

lesson_id
String
S26
Foreign key to LessonContext.lesson_id. All AlignmentLog rows produced from one lesson submission share the same lesson_id. Enables JOIN-style lookups between tabs.

logged_at
DateTime
S26
Server timestamp when S26 wrote this row. ISO 8601. Used for audit trail and as the sort key when generating coverage reports chronologically.

── DENORMALIZED LESSON FIELDS (for readable exports) ──

lesson_date
Date
S26
Copied from LessonContext at write time. Denormalized so the AlignmentLog tab is self-contained and readable without a JOIN. This is the field that appears in the end-of-term documentation report.

teacher_email
String
S26
Copied from LessonContext. Denormalized. Enables filtering the alignment log by teacher without looking up the lesson_id in LessonContext first.

learning_objective
Text
S26
Copied from LessonContext. The human-readable description of what this lesson was for. Makes the log row meaningful in isolation — an administrator reading the export sees what was taught, not just a competency code.

── COMPETENCY FIELDS ──

competency_id
String
S26
The single competency this row represents. Foreign key to CompetencyRegistry.competency_id. One row per competency per lesson — multiple competencies produce multiple rows.

competency_text
Text
S26
Copied from CompetencyRegistry at write time. Full competency statement. Denormalized for the same reason as learning_objective — the export document should be self-contained, not dependent on another tab.

strand
String
S26
Copied from CompetencyRegistry. Used in the coverage report to group log rows by competency domain (Writing, Reading, etc.) and identify which strands have and haven't been addressed.

◈
Why denormalize? The AlignmentLog is the export artifact. When an administrator asks for documentation of competency coverage, you want to be able to filter the AlignmentLog tab directly in Sheets and share it — not explain that they need to cross-reference two other tabs to understand what they're reading. Denormalization here is a deliberate product choice, not a schema mistake.

◈
Append-only is the rule. S26 never updates or deletes AlignmentLog rows. If a teacher resubmits a lesson context with corrected competency IDs, S26 appends new rows and notes the correction in LessonContext.error_notes. The old rows remain for audit integrity. A future admin function can filter by logged_at to use the most recent mapping per lesson_id.

#### Implementation Notes for Scripts 22 + 26

Decisions that should be made before writing a line of code.

TAB CREATION
Script 28 (setup wizard extension) should be the one that creates all three tabs with the correct headers. Scripts 22 and 26 should detect missing tabs and throw a clear error rather than attempting to create them — keeps setup concerns separated from operational concerns. For the lightweight build before S28 exists, a manual setup function in S22 is acceptable.

TRIGGER — S22
onFormSubmit trigger installed by S22's self-registering trigger pattern (same as S05 in Module 1). The Lesson Context Form's submit fires S22. S22 validates, writes to LessonContext, sets status RECEIVED, then calls S26 directly (not via a queue) — lightweight build has no need for queue decoupling at this stage.

TRIGGER — S26
Called directly by S22 after a successful LessonContext write. Also installable as a standalone time-based trigger (every 5 min) to catch any RECEIVED rows that S22 dropped on. S26 reads all LessonContext rows where alignment_logged_at is null and status is RECEIVED — idempotent, safe to run repeatedly.

VALIDATION
S22 validates: (1) lesson_date is a valid date and not in the past by more than 30 days, (2) learning_objective is non-empty, (3) every ID in competency_ids exists in CompetencyRegistry with active=TRUE. Validation failure writes status=ERROR and error_notes, and returns a user-facing error to the form response. Does not write to AlignmentLog.

DEDUP
If a teacher resubmits the same lesson (same teacher_email + lesson_date + period_or_class), S22 creates a new row with a new lesson_id rather than updating the original. The old row's status becomes SUPERSEDED — now in the enum above. S26 skips SUPERSEDED rows. This preserves history and avoids update-in-place complexity in the lightweight build.

CONFIG
Module 2 config values go in the existing _CONFIG tab (Script 19 pattern). New keys: M2_LESSON_CONTEXT_FORM_ID, M2_ENABLED, M2_DEFAULT_SUBJECT. Same getConfig_() reader — no new config infrastructure needed.

◈
Column order matters. Sheets doesn't enforce schema — the column positions in the actual tab must match what the scripts expect. Establish a canonical column order now and document it as the header row. Both S22 and S26 should write by column name (using a header lookup pattern) rather than by hardcoded column index, same pattern as Module 1.

CAS Platform · Module 2 Lightweight · Data Schema v1.0
Next: Script 22 — LessonContextHandler

Module 2 Full Build — Additional Tabs

The following tabs are created during Module 2 Full Build (warm-ups). They are not part of the Lightweight build. Full schemas in CAS_M2_WarmUp_Schema.html.

PacingGuide — 16 columns — read-only after import

20-unit pacing guide imported from PacingGuide_CAS_Context.json by Script 29. Key fields: lesson_unit_id · approx_start · approx_end · warmup_anchor · prior_lesson_connection · competency_ids per course. Drives unit resolution for shadow matrix and warm-up anchor seeds for Flow 3.

CompetencyRubrics — 8 columns — 221 rows — read-only after import

221 competency rubrics imported from CompetencyRubrics.json by Script 30. Extracted from two Canvas Common Cartridges (courses 8175 + 8177). Key fields: competency_id · demonstration_indicators (JSON array) · skill_questions (JSON array, avg 4.9 per competency). Script 24 embeds rubrics in WarmUpQueue snapshots via addRubricsToSnapshot_(). Resolves sparse context — Flow 3 always has structured skill questions regardless of lesson context detail.

TeacherMatrix — new column: competency_ids

The Teacher Matrix (Module 1, Script 16) gets one new column added by Script 31: competency_ids (comma-separated IDs, e.g. "8177-47,8177-52"). Run addCompetencyIdsColumn_() once. The teacher tags each assignment with the competencies it addresses. Script 31 reads this at 3:05am to compute per-student competency coverage from completed work artifacts.

CompetencyRubrics — July 2026 enrichment

Three new fields added to every rubric entry following extraction from both VDOE official standards files.

Field |
Coverage |
Notes |

sol_correlations |
197 / 221 |
Subject-keyed SOL code object. English: 117, History & SS: 75 (normalized from singular/plural variants), Math: 16, Science: 12. Sources: 8175 printable version (uploaded) + 8177 printable version (embedded in Canvas management cartridge at web_resources/Uploaded Media/). 24 CTSO/WBL tasks have no SOL by VDOE design. |

definition |
197 / 221 |
Official VDOE definition text (the "Demonstration includes..." block). Previously only available as demonstration_indicators array — now also present as the original prose form. |

required |
221 / 221 |
"R" for required, "O" for optional. Optional tasks carry "(O)" in the VDOE task number column. 8175: 6 optional. 8177: 6 optional. Script 31's artifact competency bridge should weight required competencies more heavily than optional ones in coverage calculations. |

PacingGuide v2 — new fields

Use PacingGuide_CAS_Context_v2.json for all imports. Rebuilt from the ten Stage lesson cards — warm-up anchors are now the full teacher-authored prompts. New fields per unit: chain_node · esports_connection · vocabulary_with_definitions (array with definitions, not just term strings). Script 29 requires a schema patch before importing v2 — the PacingGuide tab column definitions and validatePacingGuide() need updating for the new fields.

CAS · Module 2 · Warm-Up

Overview
Scoring
